# NEW UNREVIEWED retained proof; QEP conditional

# Normal rational-quotient EP supplies the remaining all-Reeb realization gate

This is a new, independently UNREVIEWED interface argument. It does not grant the pending P16.1 proofs, close P13.2, or enlarge the actual accepted round79/81 theorem. That theorem presently requires one smooth rational projective quotient. The observation below replaces its smoothness input by an explicit envelope-property premise on one actual normal quotient, and reconstructs the application rather than inferring completeness from an I-Cauchy sequence.

## 1. The exact conditional premise and domain

Retain the original normal good polarized affine cone X=Spec R over an arbitrary algebraically closed trivially valued characteristic-zero field k, its specified torus T, its invariant carrier K=X_0^T, and every fixed rational or irrational Reeb vector xi. Write n=dim X, p=n-1. Actual H, CPSH, PSH and E1, energies, probability MA, root maps and rational calibration have their exact accepted original P13.1 and round73/76 meanings. Round79/81 grants only its precise partial result and universal root/topology lemmas, not singular compactness.

For n>=2 choose a rational Reeb vector eta in the open rational cone defined by the finitely many strictly positive generator-weight inequalities. After removing its supported degree gcd, write eta=s eta_e with effective integral grading eta_e and s>0. The accepted normal-Veronese construction gives an integral normal projective quotient Y=Proj R_eta_e, a sufficiently divisible actual ample line bundle L_d=O_Y(d), residual torification q, compact B=q(Y^an), and the calibrated homeomorphism ell:B->K. No smoothness of Y is inferred.

The only additional premise for this cone is:

**QEP:** the actual class c1(L_d) has the BJ envelope property on this actual Y.

This premise concerns one projective class and is not claimed proved here. If the pending geometrically unibranch ample EP argument for P16.1 succeeds over arbitrary-cardinality k, it will supply QEP because this Y is normal and therefore geometrically unibranch over algebraically closed characteristic-zero k. Its exact EP conclusion and proof must be independently accepted first. A pending review, a source headline, or a scalar fiber descent is not QEP.

The cone probability and energy factors stay

MA_eta=V_eta^{-1}(ddc u)^p ddc rho_eta^+,
V_eta=s^{-n}d^{1-n}(L_d^p), omega_eta=L_d/(sd),
omega_eta^p=s V_eta, E_eta=E_Ld/(sd).

The projective probability divides raw curvature by (L_d^p), and not by V_eta. The n=1 point case is already accepted independently.

## 2. What QEP gives on the original normal quotient

BJ Theorem5.11 applies to an irreducible projective variety and a numerical class; it does not assume smoothness. Under QEP the genuine normalized projective PSH class for L_d is compact in the topology of pointwise convergence on divisorial valuations. Its proof takes a divisorial Tychonoff subnet and uses the genuine decreasing upper-tail envelopes supplied by EP, so this produces an actual potential, including over an uncountable field. It does not complete an abstract metric space.

BJ Proposition4.28, with its Lemma4.26 proof, gives continuity of chi -> sup_Y(chi-gamma) for every continuous gamma on this general projective Y. BJ Theorem7.1/Proposition7.7 gives genuine energy upper semicontinuity on the same general-variety scope. BJ Proposition9.19 gives L1(lambda) convergence for a weakly convergent PSH net with eventually bounded J and every projective finite-dual-energy probability lambda. No smoothness or inverse existence is an extra antecedent of 9.19. Its absolute-value step uses the independently justified correct identity

|a-b|=2(max(a,b)-b)-(a-b).

BJ Theorem9.20 equates finite dual energy with integrability of every actual E1 potential for irreducible projective Y and an ample class, independently of EP. Thus all projective tools used below have their actual normal-target scope; the smooth EP theorem5.20 is replaced only by QEP followed by5.11.

## 3. Torification and the genuine rational correspondence

On a generating finite FS section system for the actual L_d, split each section into its residual torus-weight components with the same coefficient. The components globally generate because vanishing of every component implies vanishing of every original section. The finite maximum equals the torified potential chi^T=chi o q and dominates chi by the ultrametric inequality. The canonical reference and actual residual linearization are preserved. This calculation uses the actual quotient and no smooth geometry.

Uniform limits give torification of genuine CPSH potentials. A genuine decreasing CPSH defining net gives chi^T for every projective PSH chi, since evaluating a decreasing limit at q(x) commutes with the limit. The trivial valuation is fixed, normalization is preserved, and energy monotonicity gives E_Ld(chi^T)>=E_Ld(chi). Hence E1 torifies to actual E1.

The unbounded rational correspondence is also literal. A cone CPSH defining net lifts to an invariant projective CPSH net and decreases at every analytic point to chi=sd u o ell o q. Conversely, torify a defining projective CPSH net for an invariant chi and lift it; its equivariant extensions decrease to an actual cone potential. The trivial valuation ensures the limits are nontrivial. Actual decreasing energy continuity extends the energy identity, including minus infinity. Actual fixed-field weighted decreasing continuity extends the calibrated mixed probability and weighted-integral identities. The measures remain supported on the closed B and K; the weighted identity retains the factor1/(sd) and the test-containing pairing minus the zero-test pairing.

These arguments justify genuine onto PSH and E1 correspondence at this fixed eta. They require no moving-Reeb unbounded-current identity and no normalization of Y.

## 4. Compactness and bounded-energy L1 at this normal quotient

Use the universal round79 supremum-test topology tau_eta on normalized actual cone PSH, defined by S_psi(u)=sup_K(u-psi) for actual CPSH tests. Its Hausdorff property and energy upper semicontinuity are granted only in their accepted universal scope.

Define

A(chi)=(sd)^{-1} chi^T o ell^{-1} on K.

Section3 gives actual membership and surjectivity. For a cone CPSH test psi let Psi=sd psi o ell o q. The literal identity

S_psi(A chi)=(sd)^{-1}sup_Y(chi-Psi)

holds because restriction to B and chi^T>=chi give the two opposite inequalities. Proposition4.28 makes A continuous. QEP compactness from Section2 and the Hausdorff target therefore give compactness of normalized actual cone PSH at eta. No closedness theorem for a torus-invariant weak subset is assumed.

Let u_i->u in tau_eta with J_eta(u_i)<=C eventually. Energy upper semicontinuity gives actual u in E1. For a finite-energy cone probability lambda on K, push it through ell^{-1} to a projective probability lambda_Y supported on B. Every actual projective E1 chi torifies to E1 and agrees with chi^T on B. Genuine cone integrability therefore implies chi in L1(lambda_Y). The general-variety Theorem9.20 gives finite projective dual energy of lambda_Y.

If cone L1 convergence failed, take a subnet whose distances stay above a positive constant. The invariant projective lifts chi_i=sd u_i o ell o q have bounded projective J=sd J_eta. QEP compactness gives a weakly convergent further subnet with actual limit chi. Energy usc makes chi E1. Continuity of A and Hausdorffness give A chi=u. Proposition9.19 yields chi_i->chi in L1(lambda_Y), which on B is exactly sd times the alleged cone L1 failure. This contradiction proves convergence of the whole original net, for every finite-energy lambda.

Thus QEP supplies both exact missing hypotheses of the accepted universal root-transfer argument: genuine normalized compactness and bounded-J finite-target L1 continuity at one normal rational quotient.

## 5. Transfer to every fixed Reeb field and actual realization

Use only the exact accepted round79/81 universal root homeomorphism, root energy comparison, finite-energy orbit pushforward, and finite-grid/tail L1 lemma. They apply on every original cone with their stated antecedents. For fixed xi, inverse roots transfer compactness and bounded J to eta; the orbit pushforwards provide each finite-energy target lambda_r. Section4 verifies every L1 hypothesis required by the finite-grid lemma. That lemma takes the finite grid before the net limit and the unbounded tail cutoff last. It yields L1 continuity at xi without identifying MA across Reeb fields.

The accepted round73/76 intrinsic target criterion and midpoint estimate supply a normalized actual H(xi) maximizing sequence for

L_xi(mu)=sup_{h in H(xi)}(E_xi(h)-integral_K h dmu)<infinity

and its eventually bounded J. Actual compactness gives an actual PSH subnet limit w; energy usc puts w in actual E1; target L1 continuity makes w an attained maximizer. The accepted fixed-xi outer derivative for both signs of a dense continuous direction then identifies its actual calibrated operator: MA_xi(w)=mu as probability Radon measures on the original K. Uniqueness modulo constants and necessity follow from the accepted embedding and tangent identity. Every normalized actual E1 maximizing sequence satisfies the accepted midpoint bound I_xi(w_j,w)<=d_j/(2a_p), a_p=p/4^{p+1}, and hence converges to this actual w in I. No I-completeness theorem is used to create w.

Conditional on QEP, the exact image on this original cone is therefore the positive Radon probabilities on K having finite L_xi(mu), equivalently integrating every actual E1(xi) potential, for every fixed rational or irrational xi. For p=1 the bound is I<=8d. For n=1 the accepted effective normal grading gives R=k[z] and the one-point probability; no a_0 estimate is used.

## 6. The dependency that remains

If every actual normal projective quotient over every retained k has QEP in its actual L_d, the argument applies to every original normal good cone. The pending P16.1 countable first-membership/fiber-descent route, followed by the accepted conditional countable-to-arbitrary reduction86, is one proposed supplier. Neither its pending review88 nor the fresh corrected C1 review89 is granted here. This interface itself also needs an independent mathematical review before use as a theorem.

# 1. Exact disposition and the new realization scope

The unchanged original P13.2 remains open in this deliverable. The flag is `candidate=false`. Its original domain is every normal good polarized affine cone over every algebraically closed trivially valued characteristic-zero field, with the specified torus, every rational or irrational Reeb field, actual finite-energy potentials modulo constants, the invariant carrier $K=X_0^T$, and the actual affine-cone probability normalization. The exact problem, completion criterion, full assignment, accepted proof bodies and reviews are preserved in `input-original.json`, `task-original.json`, and `assignment-original.txt`.

There is a new **actual realization theorem on a specified algebraic subdomain**, rather than a completion element or an approximate equation. Suppose there is at least one rational Reeb field $\eta$ for which the effective projective quotient
\[
Y=\operatorname{Proj}R_{\eta_e}
\]
is smooth. The integer field $\eta_e$ here has effective supported degree gcd one, and $\eta=s\eta_e$, $s>0$. Then, for **every** Reeb field $\xi$ on this same cone, including nonproportional irrational fields, the image of the genuine operator is exactly
\[
\left\{\mu\text{ positive Radon on }K:\ \mu(K)=1,\quad
L_\xi(\mu):=\sup_{h\in H(\xi)}\left(E_\xi(h)-\int_Kh\,d\mu\right)<\infty\right\}.                 \tag{1.1}
\]
Equivalently, every actual $\mathcal E^1(\xi)$ potential is $\mu$-integrable. The solution is an actual normalized potential, unique modulo constants. In particular this theorem covers **all original normal cones of dimension at most two**, over every retained field and at every Reeb field. It also covers higher-dimensional cones with a smooth rational quotient. No toric assumption is made in that theorem.

The extra smooth-quotient hypothesis is not automatic in dimension at least three; Section 9 gives a normal good cone outside it. Thus (1.1) is not asserted as a sufficient condition on every original cone. The strongest new ingredients are an actual topology on the genuine potential class, a finite-grid and tail proof of $L^1$ continuity of nonlinear orbit roots, and an actual variational limit with its energy and operator identified. None of the unreviewed root80--83 or round75 results is used.

# 2. Granted mathematical inputs and calibration

We use the supplied **full accepted P13.1 mathematical proof**, together with its exact original PASS, only in its granted scope: actual $H$, CPSH, PSH and normalized $\mathcal E^1$ classes; intrinsic raw root maps; fixed-field convexity, positivity, Hodge calculus, weighted decreasing-net continuity; calibrated rational projective correspondence; energy comparisons; and uniform outer differentiation. We use the supplied **full accepted round73 partial proof**, with exact round76 PASS, for the finite-target/integrability criterion, its field independence, genuine maximizing sequences, the deficit bound on $J$, the midpoint estimate, and optimality of an already actual maximizer. Those results do not include compactness or attainment.

For clarity, $n=\dim X\ge1$, $p=n-1$, $\rho_\xi|_K=0$, and
\[
\operatorname{MA}_\xi(u_1,\ldots,u_p)
 =V_\xi^{-1}dd^cu_1\cdots dd^cu_p\,dd^c\rho_\xi^+,
\qquad \rho_\xi^+=\max(\rho_\xi,0).                                      \tag{2.1}
\]
Its mass on $K$ is one. For continuous potentials,
\[
E_\xi(u)=\frac1{nV_\xi}\sum_{j=0}^p\int_Ku\,(dd^cu)^j
                 (dd^c\rho_\xi)^{p-j}dd^c\rho_\xi^+;                    \tag{2.2}
\]
on actual PSH it is the infimum over dominating actual CPSH potentials. Normalization means $u(v_{\rm triv})=\sup_Ku=0$, and $J_\xi(u)=-E_\xi(u)$.

For homogeneous algebra generators $h_i$ of weights $\beta_i$, set
\[
a=\min_i\frac{\langle\xi,\beta_i\rangle}{\langle\eta,\beta_i\rangle}>0,
\qquad b=\max_i\frac{\langle\xi,\beta_i\rangle}{\langle\eta,\beta_i\rangle}.
\]
Every actual $\eta$-potential satisfies, for $t\ge0$,
\[
u(v)-bt\le u(v+t\xi)\le u(v)-at.                              \tag{2.3}
\]
The same bounds after reversing the orbit imply that a finite orbit is Lipschitz and strictly decreasing, and an infinite-valued orbit is identically $-\infty$. Its zero parameter is $\mathcal R_{\eta\xi}u(v)$. The raw maps preserve order, CPSH and decreasing actual PSH nets; are inverses; and preserve normalization. For normalized actual finite-energy potentials they are onto, with the granted comparison
\[
e^{-(2n+1)\delta}J_\eta(u)\le
J_\xi(\mathcal R_{\eta\xi}u)\le e^{(2n+1)\delta}J_\eta(u),
\quad \delta=\max_i\left|\log\frac{\langle\xi,\beta_i\rangle}{\langle\eta,\beta_i\rangle}\right|.  \tag{2.4}
\]
On $K$, normalized roots satisfy $u/a\le\mathcal R u\le u/b\le0$. The finite-energy target class on the fixed $K$ is independent of the Reeb field, by the granted integrability criterion and these pointwise bounds.

For a rational $\eta=s\eta_e$, choose a sufficiently divisible Veronese $d$ and the actual ample line bundle $L_d=\mathcal O_Y(d)$. The accepted calibration is
\[
V_\eta=s^{-n}d^{1-n}(L_d^p),\quad \omega_\eta=L_d/(sd),
\quad (\omega_\eta^p)=sV_\eta,\quad E_\eta(u)=E_{L_d}(\varphi)/(sd).       \tag{2.5}
\]
The projective probability is raw projective curvature divided by $(L_d^p)$. Its calibrated Gauss lift is the cone probability; raw projective mass is not identified with $V_\eta$. Section 7 verifies the unbounded fixed-field application of (2.5).

# 3. A topology on actual potentials and actual root homeomorphisms

Write $\mathcal P_\theta$ for normalized **actual** PSH restrictions on $K$. For each actual $\psi\in\mathrm{CPSH}(\theta)$ define
\[
S_\psi(u)=\sup_K(u-\psi)\in\mathbb R.
\]
It is finite: $u\le0$, $u(v_{\rm triv})=0$, and $\psi$ is bounded on $K$. Let $\tau_\theta$ be the coarsest topology making every $S_\psi$ continuous. This introduces a topology on the given functions, not a new potential cone.

**Lemma 3.1.** $\tau_\theta$ is Hausdorff, $E_\theta$ is upper semicontinuous in it, and every normalized raw-root bijection is a homeomorphism
\[
\mathcal R_{\eta\xi}:(\mathcal P_\eta,\tau_\eta)\longrightarrow
                     (\mathcal P_\xi,\tau_\xi).                         \tag{3.1}
\]
This lemma holds on every original cone; it asserts no compactness in general.

For separation, if $u(x)>v(x)$ at some $x\in K$, take a genuine decreasing CPSH defining net $\psi_j\downarrow v$. Some $\psi_j(x)<u(x)$, including when $v(x)=-\infty$. Then $S_{\psi_j}(u)>0$ and $S_{\psi_j}(v)\le0$. If distinct restrictions have the opposite inequality, exchange them. Equivariance and torification determine the full potential from the restriction, so this also separates actual normalized potentials.

For upper semicontinuity, if $E_\theta(u)<c$, choose a genuine continuous majorant $\psi\ge u$ with $E_\theta(\psi)<c-\epsilon$. The neighborhood $S_\psi(v)<\epsilon$ gives $v\le\psi+\epsilon$ and hence $E_\theta(v)\le E_\theta(\psi)+\epsilon<c$. This works also at energy $-\infty$. Thus $J_\theta$ is lower semicontinuous on the normalized class.

We prove (3.1) using strict global order, rather than pointwise convergence on a selected valuation set. For $\psi\in\mathrm{CPSH}(\xi)$ and $t\in\mathbb R$, put
\[
g_t=\mathcal R_{\xi\eta}(\psi+t)\in\mathrm{CPSH}(\eta).
\]
Raw order equivalence, including unbounded values, gives
\[
S_\psi(\mathcal R_{\eta\xi}u)\le t\ \Longleftrightarrow\ S_{g_t}(u)\le0.   \tag{3.2}
\]
There is also equivalence with strict $<$ on the left and strict $<0$ on the right. Indeed a uniform negative gap on one side remains a uniform negative gap after applying the inverse root: by (2.3), a constant shift $c>0$ changes every root between $c/b$ and $c/a$. The reverse map has degree ratios $1/b,1/a$. Therefore strictly negative suprema cannot become zero. Strict $>t$ corresponds to strict $>0$ by the non-strict equivalence. Both strict sublevel and strict superlevel preimages in (3.2) are open in $\tau_\eta$, proving continuity. Apply the same argument to the inverse. No strong $I$ continuity is inferred.

# 4. Orbit pushforwards remain genuine finite-energy targets

Fix $\eta,\xi$ and a real $r$, and define a continuous map on the same actual carrier
\[
T_r(x)=s_\eta(x+r\xi),\qquad x\in K.                             \tag{4.1}
\]
Here $s_\eta(v)=v+\rho_\eta(v)\eta$ is the accepted radial retraction. Continuity follows from the actual torified action and the finite reference maximum. No inverse or group law for $T_r$ is asserted.

**Lemma 4.1.** If $\mu$ is a finite-energy probability on $K$, then $\mu_r=(T_r)_*\mu$ is also such a probability, for every fixed $r$. For every actual $\eta$-potential $u$ finite at $x$,
\[
|u(T_r x)-u(x)|\le2b|r|.                                      \tag{4.2}
\]
Infinite values are preserved along these maps.

To prove it, reverse (2.3) when $r<0$ and apply it directly when $r\ge0$. This gives $|u(x+r\xi)-u(x)|\le b|r|$. Applying the same estimate to the reference, which is zero at $x\in K$, gives $|\rho_\eta(x+r\xi)|\le b|r|$. Actual equivariance says
\[
u(T_r x)=u(x+r\xi)-\rho_\eta(x+r\xi),
\]
which proves (4.2). If $u(x)=-\infty$, the entire orbit is $-\infty$ by (2.3), and subtracting the finite reference preserves it. Every actual $\mathcal E^1(\eta)$ potential is $\mu$-integrable; (4.2) makes it $\mu_r$-integrable. The granted finite-target criterion now puts $\mu_r$ in the actual finite-energy target class. Positivity, Radon regularity and mass one follow from continuous pushforward on compact Hausdorff $K$. This reasoning uses actual integrability, not an unproved pullback rule for MA.

# 5. A finite-grid and tail proof of nonlinear-root $L^1$ continuity

**Lemma 5.1.** Let $u_i,u\in\mathcal E^1(\eta)$ be normalized actual potentials and let $\mu$ have finite energy. Suppose
\[
u_i\longrightarrow u\text{ in }L^1(\mu),\qquad
u_i\longrightarrow u\text{ in }L^1(\mu_r)\quad\text{for every rational }r\le0.  \tag{5.1}
\]
Then the actual normalized roots satisfy
\[
\mathcal R_{\eta\xi}u_i\longrightarrow\mathcal R_{\eta\xi}u
                       \text{ in }L^1(\mu).                           \tag{5.2}
\]
This holds for arbitrary nets, every original cone, and fixed rational or irrational fields. The hypotheses of (5.1) will be proved, rather than assumed, on the smooth-quotient subdomain in Section 6.

Put $R_i=\mathcal R_{\eta\xi}u_i$, $R=\mathcal R_{\eta\xi}u$. On $\mu$-almost every point relevant to any fixed comparison, the orbit functions
\[
G_i(r,x)=u_i(x+r\xi)=u_i(T_r x)+\rho_\eta(x+r\xi),\qquad G(r,x)=u(x+r\xi)
\]
are finite. Their slopes lie between $-b$ and $-a$. The null sets may depend on $i$; each estimate below concerns only finitely many functions for that $i$, so no common null set for an uncountable net is needed. Since $G_i(0,x)=u_i(x)\le0$, the roots are nonpositive and
\[
0\le -R_i(x)\le -u_i(x)/a,
\qquad 0\le -R(x)\le -u(x)/a.                              \tag{5.3}
\]

Fix a positive rational $M$ and a rational mesh $h=M/N$. On $[-M,0]$ use the finite grid $r_j=-M+jh$, $0\le j\le N$. The orbit Lipschitz bound gives
\[
\sup_{-M\le r\le0}|G_i(r,x)-G(r,x)|
 \le\max_j|G_i(r_j,x)-G(r_j,x)|+2bh.
\]
For two decreasing functions with downward slope at least $a$, inverse zero parameters, clipped to $[-M,0]$, differ by at most their sup-norm difference divided by $a$. To check boundary cases, if a clipped root is $-M$, the value at $-M$ is nonpositive; if the other clipped root is interior, its slope inequality bounds their distance by the discrepancy at $-M$. The same argument at zero and at two interior roots proves the assertion. Thus, writing $R_i^M=\max(R_i,-M)$,
\[
\int|R_i^M-R^M|\,d\mu
 \le a^{-1}\sum_{j=0}^N\|u_i-u\|_{L^1(\mu_{r_j})}+2bh/a.                \tag{5.4}
\]
The finite reference terms cancel. First let the net index tend to its limit with this finite grid fixed; then let $N\to\infty$. This proves clipped $L^1$ convergence for each fixed $M$.

The actual tail estimate, stronger than a bound on norms, is
\[
\int|R_i-R_i^M|\,d\mu\le a^{-1}\int(-u_i-aM)_+\,d\mu.                  \tag{5.5}
\]
It follows pointwise from (5.3). For fixed $M$, the right-hand integral converges to the one with $u$, since $z\mapsto(-z-aM)_+$ is 1-Lipschitz and (5.1) holds at $r=0$. Combining (5.4)--(5.5),
\[
\limsup_i\|R_i-R\|_{L^1(\mu)}
 \le \frac2a\int(-u-aM)_+\,d\mu.                               \tag{5.6}
\]
The last quantity tends to zero by integrability of the fixed actual $u$. This proves (5.2), with tail control and the order of limits explicit. It does not exchange an unbounded varying test with a weakly convergent measure and does not claim an unbounded moving-Reeb mixed-current theorem.

# 6. Genuine compactness and bounded-energy $L^1$ continuity at a smooth rational quotient

For Sections 6--8 assume $n\ge2$; the $n=1$ point case is treated directly at the end of Section 8. Assume now that $Y$ for the rational field $\eta$ is smooth. The accepted rational construction supplies the actual $L_d$, its canonical reference, a continuous residual torification $q:Y^{\rm an}\to Y^{\rm an}$, the compact carrier $B=q(Y^{\rm an})$, and the calibrated homeomorphism $\ell:B\to K$. All torification formulas use the actual residual linearization and eigen-section charts in the granted P13.1 proof.

For every genuine projective PSH potential $\varphi$, define
\[
\varphi^T=\varphi\circ q.
\]
This is actual PSH and $\varphi^T\ge\varphi$. On FS metrics, split sections into their residual weight components with the same additive coefficients. The components still globally generate, the maximum is $\varphi\circ q$, and the ultrametric inequality gives the domination. Uniform approximation gives the statement for CPSH. For a decreasing genuine CPSH defining net for $\varphi$, its torifications decrease pointwise to $\varphi\circ q$, establishing actual PSH membership. The trivial valuation is fixed, so normalization is preserved. Monotonicity gives
\[
E_{L_d}(\varphi^T)\ge E_{L_d}(\varphi),                         \tag{6.1}
\]
and in particular torification preserves actual finite energy.

We apply established smooth projective theorems in the authorized primary C, *Global pluripotential theory over a trivially valued field*, arXiv:1801.08229v4. Its hypotheses are checked individually here: $k$ is algebraically closed, trivially valued and characteristic zero; $Y$ is an actual integral smooth projective variety, hence connected; and $L_d$ is an actual ample line bundle. C Theorem 5.20 and Corollary 5.21 give weak compactness of $\mathrm{PSH}_{\rm sup}(L_d)$. C Proposition 4.28 gives continuity of $\varphi\mapsto\sup(\varphi-\gamma)$ for every continuous $\gamma$; Proposition 7.7 gives energy upper semicontinuity. C Proposition 9.19 gives $L^1(\lambda)$ convergence for weakly converging genuine PSH nets with bounded $J$ and any finite-energy projective probability $\lambda$.

The source's compactness is an actual decreasing-net argument: Tychonoff gives a subnet of divisorial values; genuine upper tail envelopes, furnished by smooth EP, decrease to an actual PSH potential. Nonnegligibility of divisorial points identifies its values and excludes the identically $-\infty$ limit. This works over uncountable $k$ without metrizability. The smooth EP proof uses multiplier-ideal subadditivity and uniform global generation on a smooth test model to obtain uniform envelope approximation. We apply that published primary theorem on the smooth $Y$ only. No singular envelope conclusion of E is imported.

**Proposition 6.1.** $(\mathcal P_\eta,\tau_\eta)$ is compact. If $u_i\to u$ in this topology with $J_\eta(u_i)$ eventually bounded, then $u\in\mathcal E^1(\eta)$ and $u_i\to u$ in $L^1(\lambda)$ for every finite-energy cone probability $\lambda$ on $K$.

Define a map from the genuine compact projective normalized class to the genuine cone class by
\[
A(\varphi)=\frac1{sd}\,\varphi^T\circ\ell^{-1}\big|_K.                  \tag{6.2}
\]
Its actual membership and surjectivity are justified in Section 7. If $\psi$ is a cone CPSH test, put $\Psi=sd\,\psi\circ\ell\circ q$, a genuine invariant projective CPSH potential. Then
\[
S_\psi(A\varphi)=\frac1{sd}\sup_{Y^{\rm an}}(\varphi-\Psi).             \tag{6.3}
\]
Indeed the supremum of $\varphi^T-\Psi$ is attained as a supremum on $B$; it is bounded above by the full supremum of $\varphi-\Psi$. The domination $\varphi^T\ge\varphi$ gives the reverse inequality. C Proposition 4.28 makes (6.3) weakly continuous. Thus (6.2) is a continuous surjection from a compact space onto the Hausdorff space of Section 3, proving compactness. We need neither closedness of the invariant subset in the projective weak topology nor weak continuity of roots on all divisorial valuations.

Upper semicontinuity from Lemma 3.1 gives $E_\eta(u)\ge- C$ if the eventual $J$ bound is $C$, so $u$ is actual finite energy. To prove $L^1$, regard each $u_i$ as the invariant projective potential $\varphi_i=sd\,u_i\circ\ell\circ q$. Its normalized projective $J$ is $sdJ_\eta(u_i)$. Push $\lambda$ to a probability $\lambda_Y$ supported on $B$. It has genuine projective finite energy: for every projective $\chi\in\mathcal E^1(L_d)$, (6.1) gives an invariant finite-energy $\chi^T$, whose cone restriction is an actual $\eta$-E1 potential. On $B$, $\chi=\chi^T$. The granted cone integrability criterion gives $\chi\in L^1(\lambda_Y)$; C Theorem 9.20 then gives finite projective dual energy.

If $L^1(\lambda)$ convergence failed, take a subnet on which its distance stays above a fixed positive number, and then a weakly convergent projective subnet $\varphi_i\to\chi$. Energy upper semicontinuity makes $\chi$ finite energy. Continuity of (6.2) and uniqueness of the $\tau_\eta$ limit imply $A\chi=u$. Since $\chi=\chi^T$ on $B$, the projective C9.19 conclusion
\[
\varphi_i\longrightarrow\chi\quad\hbox{in }L^1(\lambda_Y)
\]
contradicts the supposed cone $L^1$ failure after division by $sd$. This proves convergence of the whole original net.

We retain C9.19's actual proof and its hypotheses. Its first assertion follows from uniform approximation of target integration on bounded-$J$ sets by integration against genuine bounded model MA measures, whose pairings are weakly continuous. For the absolute $L^1$ assertion, use $\chi_i'=\max(\chi_i,\chi)$ and the corrected identity
\[
|\chi_i-\chi|=2\max(\chi_i,\chi)-\chi_i-\chi.
\]
These maxima converge on divisorial values, remain normalized, and have bounded $J$ by energy monotonicity. Applying the first assertion twice proves $L^1$. The misprinted identity at C LF5254 is not used. No cone absolute $L^1$ assertion is obtained merely from the accepted signed estimate; the projective compactness and this proof are the new inputs.

# 7. Actual rational correspondence and all-Reeb compactness

We justify the genuine unbounded correspondence used in (6.2). At the continuous level it is the accepted primary/eigen-section quotient correspondence, with $\varphi=sd\,u\circ\ell\circ q$ and the energy equality (2.5). Given actual cone $u$ and a defining CPSH net $u_j\downarrow u$, its projective images decrease to the displayed $\varphi$, at every analytic point after composition with $q$. Normalization guarantees a finite value at the trivial valuation. Consequently $\varphi$ is actual projective PSH, rather than a weak completion element.

Conversely, for an invariant projective $\varphi$, take any actual decreasing CPSH defining net $\varphi_j\downarrow\varphi$ and torify it. The actual continuous invariant metrics $\varphi_j^T$ decrease to $\varphi$. The continuous correspondence lifts them to genuine cone CPSH metrics. Their restrictions and equivariant extensions decrease pointwise on the real cone carrier and the punctured cone, including all $-\infty$ values. Thus their nontrivial limit is genuine cone PSH. The compact directed-refinement argument in C Lemma 4.6, or the granted actual decreasing closure, handles a decreasing net of already PSH members; no sequential regularization of an uncountable space is required.

Decreasing continuity of both genuine energies now extends (2.5) to these unbounded potentials, in both directions, including energy $-\infty$. Thus correspondence is onto actual E1 as well. At this rational field, weighted mixed-operator identities extend by actual fixed-field decreasing approximants. Write $\varphi_i=sd\,u_i\circ\ell\circ q$ for an actual E1 tuple, and let $m_{L_d}(\varphi_1,\ldots,\varphi_p)$ denote the genuine projective mixed probability. Then
\[
\ell_*m_{L_d}(\varphi_1,\ldots,\varphi_p)
       =\operatorname{MA}_\eta(u_1,\ldots,u_p),\qquad
\int_Ku_0\,d\operatorname{MA}_\eta
       =\frac1{sd}\int_B\varphi_0\,dm_{L_d}.                    \tag{7.0}
\]
The measures on the left are supported on $B$, so $\ell_*$ is defined. For a simultaneous decreasing CPSH defining net these are the bounded calibrated Gauss/finite-map identities at every stage. The granted cone weighted continuity and the genuine projective weighted decreasing-net theorem C7.21 pass the measures and weighted integrals to the unbounded limits. The latter follows from convergent mixed energy pairings with the test-containing pairing **minus** the zero-test pairing, followed by the positive weighted-measure mass argument; the reversed displayed sign in its printed proof is not used. This is a fixed-$\eta$ passage, not a Reeb approximation. Support passes through the closed $B$ and $K$, mass remains one, and the factors (2.5) are retained.

For an arbitrary fixed $\xi$, Lemma 3.1 and Proposition 6.1 give compactness of the actual normalized $(\mathcal P_\xi,\tau_\xi)$ via the genuine root bijection. If $w_i\to w$ in $\tau_\xi$ and $J_\xi(w_i)$ is bounded, energy upper semicontinuity puts $w$ in actual E1. The inverse roots $u_i=\mathcal R_{\xi\eta}w_i$ converge in $\tau_\eta$ and have bounded $J_\eta$ by (2.4). For every finite-energy probability $\mu$ on $K$, Lemma 4.1 puts every $\mu_r$ in that class. Proposition 6.1 supplies all the $L^1$ hypotheses (5.1). Lemma 5.1 therefore proves
\[
w_i\longrightarrow w\quad\hbox{in }L^1(\mu)                          \tag{7.1}
\]
for every such target, with no rationality assumption on $\xi$. In particular we have an actual common-carrier compactness limit, bounded energy, explicit unbounded tails, and actual finite-energy membership. We have not moved unbounded MA currents across varying Reeb fields.

# 8. Attainment and genuine operator identification

Let $\mu$ satisfy (1.1), and fix any rational or irrational $\xi$ under the smooth-quotient hypothesis. The granted exact finite-target theorem identifies $L_\xi(\mu)$ with the full E1 dual supremum and with integrability of every actual E1 potential. Choose normalized **actual FS** maximizing $w_j$, with deficit
\[
d_j=L_\xi(\mu)-E_\xi(w_j)+\int_Kw_j\,d\mu\longrightarrow0.
\]
For $p\ge1$ the granted midpoint bound gives
\[
J_\xi(w_j)\le \frac{L_\xi(\mu)+d_j}{2a_p},\qquad a_p=p/4^{p+1}.        \tag{8.1}
\]
This bound is an input with its precise accepted scope, not a new repeated signed estimate.

Actual compactness gives a subnet converging in $\tau_\xi$ to an actual normalized PSH potential $w$. Upper semicontinuity and (8.1) give $E_\xi(w)>-\infty$. Equation (7.1) gives target $L^1$ convergence. Therefore
\[
E_\xi(w)-\int_Kw\,d\mu
 \ge \limsup_j\left(E_\xi(w_j)-\int_Kw_j\,d\mu\right)=L_\xi(\mu).       \tag{8.2}
\]
The opposite inequality is the defining dual supremum on actual E1. Hence $w$ is an actual maximizer.

Here is the exact operator identification. For the granted outer functional $F_\xi^\downarrow$, every bounded-above integrable usc obstacle $h$ satisfies
\[
F_\xi^\downarrow(h)-\int_Kh\,d\mu\le L_\xi(\mu).
\]
It follows by using feasible actual CPSH competitors for continuous obstacles, and then a decreasing net of continuous majorants on compact $K$. Directedness and Radon regularity pass their integrals to the limit; metrizability is unnecessary. Apply it to $h=w+tf$, where $f$ is in the granted dense continuous direction lattice. The actual maximizer has deficit zero, so the two signs of the granted derivative
\[
F_\xi^\downarrow(w+tf)=E_\xi(w)+t\int_Kf\,d\operatorname{MA}_\xi(w)+O_f(t^2)
\]
give $\int_Kf\,d\operatorname{MA}_\xi(w)=\int_Kf\,d\mu$. Density and the actual mass-one probabilities yield
\[
\operatorname{MA}_\xi(w)=\mu.                                         \tag{8.3}
\]
The differentiated operator is the genuine fixed-$\xi$ operator of (2.1), defined on the actual E1 potential just constructed. No measure root-map identity or abstract completion is substituted for it. Its weighted decreasing-net definition is the granted one, and Section 7 has checked the only new unbounded rational correspondence application.

The existing bi-Lipschitz embedding gives uniqueness modulo constants. Necessity is the granted genuine tangent identity $L_\xi(\operatorname{MA}_\xi(w))=E_\xi(w)-\int_Kw\,d\operatorname{MA}_\xi(w)<\infty$, together with positivity, support on $K$ and probability mass one. This proves both directions of (1.1) on the stated subdomain.

There is also genuine realization of the **whole maximizing sequence**. The midpoint estimate against the actual maximizer gives
\[
I_\xi(w_j,w)\le d_j/(2a_p)\longrightarrow0.                           \tag{8.4}
\]
The granted target-integral and energy convergence, or (7.1) and uniqueness of all compact cluster points, identifies their limits with $\int w\,d\mu$ and $E_\xi(w)$. Thus the I-Cauchy sequence is realized by this actual $w$, not merely by an isometry class in a completion.

For $p=1$, $a_1=1/16$, so (8.4) reads $I(w_j,w)\le8d_j$. No exponent-zero estimate or denominator $p-1$ is needed in the new $L^1$ proof. For $n=1$, the granted normal effective grading gives $R=k[z]$ and $K=\{v_{\rm triv}\}$. Energy is $E(c)=c$, the empty horizontal product has the calibrated radial unit mass, and the sole probability is realized. We do not use (8.1) with $a_0$.

# 9. Exact algebraic reach and the remaining original gap

When $n=2$, every rational effective quotient is an integral normal projective curve, by the accepted normal-Veronese construction. At each closed point its one-dimensional normal local ring is a DVR and therefore regular. Over the perfect field $k$, the regular curve is smooth: equivalently, in an affine presentation the cotangent space has dimension one, the Jacobian has rank equal to the codimension at every closed point, and the smooth locus contains all closed points and hence is the whole finite-type curve. The dimension-zero quotient for $n=1$ is a point. This proves the claimed all-cone result for $n\le2$ without adding smoothness to the original cone.

For higher dimension the smooth-quotient hypothesis can fail. Take the specified torus $T=\mathbb G_m$ acting by ordinary positive degree on
\[
R=k[x_0,x_1,x_2,x_3]/(x_0x_1-x_2^2).
\]
This ring is the invariant subring $k[a,b,c]^{\{\pm1\}}$, where the involution sends $(a,b,c)$ to $(-a,-b,c)$, with $x_0=a^2,x_1=b^2,x_2=ab,x_3=c$. It is a normal domain: a finite group invariant subring of a normal domain is normal, by the integrality and invariant-fraction-field argument already granted in P13.1. It has dimension three, degree-zero part $k$, and is a normal good affine cone for this scalar torus. Its projective quotient is the quadric cone
\[
Y=\{x_0x_1=x_2^2\}\subset\mathbb P^3.
\]
At $[0:0:0:1]$, its local affine equation is $uv=w^2$; the cotangent space has dimension three whereas the local dimension is two. Thus $Y$ is singular. Every rational Reeb field in this specified one-dimensional torus yields the same Proj, since scaling and Veronese do not alter it. This example only demonstrates that the theorem's additional algebraic hypothesis is absent on part of the original domain. It is not a nonexistence counterexample.

To finish original P13.2 one still needs genuine normalized compactness, or another actual realization theorem, for normal cones without such a smooth rational quotient. The present topology and root-tail lemma reduce the moving-Reeb issue: if genuine bounded-energy compactness and finite-energy target $L^1$ continuity were independently established at one normal rational quotient, Sections 3--8 would transfer actual realization to every fixed Reeb field. But that singular compactness premise is not established here, and this conditional extension is not labeled an original solution.

In particular the unreviewed round75 bounded products and norm traces do not supply compact signed IBP, smooth weak/global equality or singular EP. The new printed Wu envelope conclusion is not assumed. C Theorem 12.8 explicitly equates projective EP, surjectivity and E1 completeness; it cannot turn the previously accepted I-Cauchy estimate into genuine membership without a new geometric input. The original higher-dimensional singular-quotient gap is indispensable. All results here are unverified mathematical drafts; source use and successful execution do not constitute independent validation.

      "extract_sha256": "91cb6971ff8f4d1ccb2be1c7a7cabfabe250e39b0df8f8e4d32405c510485cd7",

      "coordinate_rule": "UTF-8 bytes decode, split('\\n'); inclusive endpoints",
      "file": "01-A-LF277-346.txt",
      "literal": "Definition 2.9. A Fubini–Study function on X an with polarization ξ is a function of the\nform\n\n                                       log |fj | + λj\n                            ϕ = max {                 : fj ∈ Rαj }\n                                 1≤j≤N    hξ, αj i\n        TN\nwhere    j=1 {fj = 0} = {o}, log |fj |(v) := −v(fj ), and λj ∈ R. We will denote by H(ξ) the\nset of Fubini–Study functions on X an with polarization ξ, and write ϕξ := max{ log   |fα |\n                                                                                    hξ,αi }.\n\n    The following lemma follows directly from the definition.\nLemma 2.10. With notation as above, we have\n  (1) If ϕ ∈ H(ξ), then ϕ + c ∈ H(ξ), ∀c ∈ R;\n  (2) If ϕ1 , ϕ2 ∈ H(ξ), then max{ϕ1 , ϕ2 } ∈ H(ξ);\n  (3) If ϕ ∈ H(ξ), then aϕ ∈ H( a1 ξ) for a > 0.                                    \u0001\n  (4) If ξ is rational, and ϕ1 ∈ H(aξ), ϕ2 ∈ H(bξ), then ϕ1 + ϕ2 ∈ H ( a1 + 1b )−1 ξ =\n          ab\n      H( a+b  ξ).\n  Given a FS function ϕ ∈ H(ξ), if ξi → ξ, then we get a sequence of ϕi ∈ H(ξi ) by only\nchanging the denominator from ξ to ξi .\nLemma 2.11. With the notation above, we have that ϕi converges to ϕ locally uniformly\non X an \\ {0}.\nProof. Fix a compact subset K ⊂ X an \\ {0}. Without loss of generality, we can assume all\nlog |fj | are finite valued on K. Indeed, if log |fj | can take −∞ on K for some j, then we\ncan write K = K1 ∪ K2 where K1 = K ∩ {v(fj ) ≥ −M } and K2 = K ∩ {v(fj ) ≤ −M } for\nsome M ≪ minK ϕ. On K1 all log |fj | are finite valued, and on K2 we can drop the term\ninvolving log |fj | in our expression of ϕ.\n\f     SOME NON-ARCHIMEDEAN PLURIPOTENTIAL THEORY ON POLARIZED AFFINE CONES                       7\n\n\n  Now it suffices to show that if log |f | is finite valued on K, then ψi = loghξ|f |+λ\n                                                                                 i ,αi\n                                                                                        converges\nto ψ = loghξ,αi\n           |f |+λ\n                  uniformly on K. This is because\n                                                           1       1\n                     |ψi − ψ| ≤ sup(| log |f | + λ|)|           −       |→0\n                                  K                     hξi , αi hξ, αi\nuniformly.                                                                                     \u0003\n   As observed in [Wu22], the class of Fubini–Study functions are in particular psh-approachable\nin the sense of Chambert–Loir and Ducros, whose theory allows us to build the NA Monge–\nAmpère measure:\nTheorem 2.12 ([Wu22, Corollary 5.7]). For ϕ1 , · · · , ϕn−1 ∈ H(ξ), the associated Monge–\nAmpère measure\n                                           1\n            MA(ϕ1 , · · · , ϕn−1 ; ξ) :=        d′ d′′ ϕ1 ∧ · · · ∧ d′ d′′ ϕn−1 ∧ d′ d′′ ϕ+\n                                                                                          ξ ,\n                                         vol(ξ)\nwhere ϕ+                                                        T\n       ξ := max{ϕξ , 0}, is a probability measure supported on X0 .\n\n   When ϕ = ϕ1 = · · · = ϕn−1 , and ξ is clear from context, we will simply write MA(ϕ)\nfor simplicity. We remark that the above assignment is also symmetric and multilinear.\n\n                     3. Continuous plurisubharmonic functions\n   In this section we introduce a broader class of plurisubharmonic (psh) functions than\nFS functions. Unlike the global case, where the sum of two FS functions is still a FS\nfunction; more precisely, one can add two metrics on two line bundles L1 , L2 respectively\nto get a new metric on L1 + L2 , FS functions are more rigid in the local case. First, it\nis not clear what it means to add two FS functions with non-proportional polarizations.\nWhile we believe there is some intepretation that can be made in this situation, this shall\nnot be our focus here. Thus in the sequel, we will often fix a polarization ξ. Second, when\nξ is irrational, the sum of two FS functions with the same polarization may not be a FS\nfunction anymore. Thus we need to start with a larger class: the class of continuous psh\nfunctions.\nDefinition 3.1. The space of continuous ξ-psh functions CPSH(ξ) is the closure of H(ξ)\nin the space C 0 (X an , ξ) of continuous ξ-equivariant functions with topology given by local\nuniform convergence.",
      "excerpt_sha256": "097c42b3eef0b04de18488919b137781cd83c3ec1fbbf4eb06a341350cefaffa",
      "application_and_limit": "Actual FS and CPSH definitions; primary lists and local uniform completion."
    },
    {
      "id": "e2",
      "source": "A",
      "version": "2406.13684v1",
      "extract_sha256": "91cb6971ff8f4d1ccb2be1c7a7cabfabe250e39b0df8f8e4d32405c510485cd7",
      "inclusive_one_based_LF": [
        762,
        805
      ],
      "coordinate_rule": "UTF-8 bytes decode, split('\\n'); inclusive endpoints",
      "file": "02-A-LF762-805.txt",
      "literal": "Definition 5.1. We call ϕ : X an → R ∪ {−∞} a ξ-plurisubharmonic (ξ-psh) function on\n(X an ; ξ) if ϕ is a pointwise limit of a decreasing net (ϕi ) in CPSH(ξ), and ϕ 6≡ −∞. We\ndenote by PSH(ξ) the set of ξ-psh functions on X an .\nProposition 5.2. Fix ξ on X.\n  (1) PSH(aξ) = a1 PSH(ξ) for all t ∈ R+ .\n                                               T\n   (2) PSH(ξ) is convex and C 0 (X an \\ {0}, ξ) PSH(ξ) = CPSH(ξ).\n   (3) For any decreasing net (ϕi ) in PSH(ξ) with pointwise limit ϕ, either ϕ ∈ PSH(ξ)\n       or ϕ ≡ −∞.\n\f     SOME NON-ARCHIMEDEAN PLURIPOTENTIAL THEORY ON POLARIZED AFFINE CONES                       15\n\n   (4) Every function in PSH(ξ) is the pointwise limit of a decreasing net in H(ξ).\nProof. (1) and first assertion of (2) follow from analogous result on continous psh functions.\nIt is clear that CPSH(ξ) ⊆ C 0 (X an \\ {o}, ξ) ∩ PSH(ξ). Let ϕ ∈ PSH(ξ) be a function\nthat is also continuous on X an \\ {o}. Then by definition, there is a decreasing sequence\nϕi ∈ CPSH(ξ) converging pointwise to ϕ. Then on each compact subset K ⊂ X an \\{o}, the\nconvergence is uniform by Dini’s lemma. Hence C 0 (X an \\ {o}, ξ) ∩ PSH(ξ) = CPSH(ξ). (3)\nand (4) are consequences of [BJ22, Lemma 4.6] with K = X0 , Fe = CPSH(ξ), F = H(ξ). \u0003\nCorollary 5.3. For any ϕ ∈ PSH(ξ) we have ϕ > −∞ on the set of quasi-monomial\nvaluations in X0 .\nProof. First if ϕ = log hξ,αi\n                        |fα |+λα\n                                 , then by Proposition 2.8 we have\n                                      ϕ(v) ≥ ϕ(vtriv ) − T (v; ξ).\nWe claim that the same inequality holds for ϕ ∈ PSH(ξ). If ϕ ∈ FS(ξ), then the above\ninequality still holds, since taking finite maxima does not affect it. In general, it follows\nfrom Definition-Proposition 5.2. Thus it remains to show that T (v; ξ) < ∞. To this end,\nnote that finiteness does not depend on ξ, we can choose a compactification Y ⊃ X where\nY is a normal projective variety with grading induced by some rational ξ (e.g. one can\ntake Y to be the corresponding orbifold cone for some rational ξ). Then by [BKMS15,\nProposition 2.12], v has linear growth, that is, T (v; ξ) < ∞.                             \u0003\n  We in fact have an alternative characterization of T (v) using general psh functions.\nCorollary 5.4. For any v ∈ X0 , we have\n                                                           \u0012                \u0013\n                               T (v, ξ) =         sup          sup ϕ − ϕ(v).\n                                                ϕ∈PSH(ξ)       X0\n\nProof. Denote by T the right hand side of the equality in the corollary. Let ϕ = log |fα |\n                                                                                  hξ,αi ∈\nPSH(ξ). Then ϕ(v) ≥ ϕ(vtriv ) − T (v) implies T (v, ξ) ≤ T by Definition-Proposition 2.8.\nOn the other hand, after adding constants and taking max, the same inequality holds for\nϕ ∈ FS(ξ), and hence also for ϕ ∈ PSH(ξ) by Proposition 5.2. This proves T (v; ξ) ≥ T ,\nand we are done.                                                                           \u0003\n   We equip PSH(ξ) with the topology of pointwise convergence on quasi-monomial valu-",
      "excerpt_sha256": "c3a576d9e8c0a95a6e91983e40d30177003abc920dfa62fb0f70fe4d72ac90b1",
      "application_and_limit": "Actual decreasing-net PSH definition and closure; the proof is retained but no disputed finite-quasimonomial sup-detection argument is imported."
    },
    {
      "id": "e3",
      "source": "C",
      "version": "1801.08229v4",
      "extract_sha256": "659bb8d2e5335781041f03cdb168bac70126487bc0db394f3dac8cf1e9f076d0",
      "inclusive_one_based_LF": [
        2241,

      ],
      "coordinate_rule": "UTF-8 bytes decode, split('\\n'); inclusive endpoints",
      "file": "03-C-LF2241-2290.txt",
      "literal": "Lemma 4.6. Let K be a compact topological space, and assume we are given:\n      • a set S and, for each s ∈ S, a family Fs of continuous functions f : K → R ∪ {−∞},\n         stable under addition of a constant;\n      • a map ρ : S → T to a metrizable topological space T .\nFor each t ∈ T , denote by Fet the set of all (usc) functions g : K → R ∪ {−∞} that can\nbe written as the pointwise limit of a decreasing net (fi ) with fi ∈ Fsi for a net (si ) of S\n                                          ≈\nsuch that limi ρ(si ) = t in T . Then Ft = Fet for any t ∈ T . In other words, if a function\ng : K → R ∪ {−∞} can be written as the pointwise limit of a decreasing net (gi )i∈I with\ngi ∈ Feti for a net (ti ) of T such that limi ti = t, then g ∈ Fet .\nProof. Pick a metric d on T inducing the given topology. For each i ∈ I, gi is the pointwise\nlimit of a decreasing net (fi,j )j∈Ji with fi,j ∈ Fsi,j for a net (si,j )j∈Ji in S such that ti,j :=\nρ(si,j ) satisfies limj ti,j = ti . Denote by A the set of triples α = (i, j, ε) with i ∈ I, j ∈ Ji\nand ε ∈ R>0 , and define a partial preorder on A by setting\n                                                 i ≥ i0\n                                                \n                                  0 0 0\n                    (i, j, ε) ≥ (i , j , ε ) ⇐⇒   fi,j + ε ≤ fi0 ,j 0 + ε0 on K                 (4.4)\n                                                  d(ti,j , ti ) + ε ≤ d(ti0 ,j 0 , ti0 ) + ε0 ,\n                                                \n\nWe claim that A is directed. To see this, pick α1 = (i1 , j1 , ε1 ), α2 = (i2 , j2 , ε2 ) in A. Since\nI is directed, we can choose i3 ∈ I with i3 ≥ i1 , i2 . Pick also ε3 > 0 with ε3 < min{ε1 , ε2 }.\nSince (gi )i is decreasing, we have gi3 ≤ min{gi1 , gi2 } ≤ min{fi1 ,j1 , fi2 ,j2 }, and hence\n                              gi3 + ε3 < min{fi1 ,j1 + ε1 , fi2 ,j2 + ε2 }                     (4.5)\npointwise on K. For each j ∈ Ji3 , define hj : K → R by\n                   hj := exp (fi3 ,j + ε3 ) − exp (min{fi1 ,j1 + ε1 , fi2 ,j2 + ε2 }) .\n\f              GLOBAL PLURIPOTENTIAL THEORY OVER A TRIVIALLY VALUED FIELD                                      47\n\nThen (hj ) is a decreasing net of continuous functions such that limj hj < 0 pointwise on K,\nby (4.5). The compact space K can thus be written as the increasing union of the open sets\n{hj < 0}, and hence K = {hj3 < 0} for some j3 ∈ Ji3 , i.e.\n                            fi3 ,j3 + ε3 < min{fi1 ,j1 + ε1 , fi2 ,j2 + ε2 } on K.\nSince limj ti3 ,j = ti3 and ε3 < min{ε1 , ε2 }, we can further arrange, after increasing j3 if\nneeded, that\n          d(ti3 ,j3 , ti3 ) + ε3 < min{ε1 , ε2 } ≤ min {d(ti1 ,j1 , ti1 ) + ε1 , d(ti2 ,j2 , ti2 ) + ε2 } .\nThus α3 := (i3 , j3 , ε3 ) ∈ A satisfies α3 ≥ α1 , α2 , which proves, as desired, that A is directed.\n  Now, for each α = (i, j, ε) ∈ A, set\n                  sα := si,j ∈ S,       tα = ρ(sα ) ∈ T,        and gα := fi,j + ε ∈ Fsα ,\nwhere the last containment holds because Fsα is assumed to be stable under addition of a\nconstant. Since limi gi (x) = g(x) and\nlimi ti = t, we can find i0 ∈ I such that gi0 (x) < g(x) + δ and d(ti , t) < δ for all i ≥ i0 .\nUsing limj fi0 ,j (x) = gi0 (x) and limj ti0 ,j = ti0 , we next pick j0 ∈ Ji0 and 0 < ε0 \u001c 1 such\nthat fi0 ,j0 (x) + ε0 < g(x) + δ and d(ti0 ,j0 , ti0 ) + ε0 < δ.       "literal": "Lemma 4.26. For each ψ ∈ PL(X), there exists a finite set Σ ⊂ X div such that\n                                  sup(ϕ − ψ) = max(ϕ − ψ)                                  (4.7)\n                                   X an            Σ\n\nfor all ϕ ∈ PL+ (X), and the same result then holds for all ϕ ∈ PSH(θ), θ ∈ N1 (X).\nMoreover, if ψ = r(ϕa − ρ), where r ∈ Q>0 , a is a flag ideal, and ρ ∈ PL+ (X), then we can\npick Σ = Σa , the set of Rees valuations of a.\nProof. We can always write ψ = r(ϕa − ρ) as above, and by homogeneity we may assume\nr = 1. The case ϕ ∈ PL+ (X) now follows from Lemma 2.13, since ϕ + ρ ∈ PL+ (X).\n  Now assume ϕ ∈ PSH(θ) for some θ ∈ N1 (X). After replacing θ with ω ∈ Amp(X)\nsuch that ω − θ is nef, we may assume θ = ω is ample. By Theorem 4.15, we can find a\ndecreasing net (ϕi ) in Hdom (ω) ⊂ PL+ (X) such that ϕi & ϕ. For each v ∈ X an , we then\nhave ϕi (v) − ψ(v) ≤ maxΣa (ϕi − ψ) for all i, and hence ϕ(v) − ψ(v) ≤ maxΣa (ϕ − ψ). \u0003\n\f52                      SÉBASTIEN BOUCKSOM AND MATTIAS JONSSON\n\nProof of Theorem 4.22. If ψ ∈ PL(X), the result follows from Lemma 4.26. Assume next\nψ ∈ C0 (X). By Theorem 2.2, ψ is a uniform limit of functions in PL(X), and the result\nthus holds for ψ as well. In the general case, we can find a decreasing net (ψi ) in C0 (X)\nwith ψi → ψ. Then ϕ ≤ ψ ≤ ψi on X div implies ϕ ≤ ψi on X an , and hence ϕ ≤ ψ on X an\nas well.                                                                                  \u0003\nRemark 4.27. Assume char k = 0. By Corollary A.3, any v ∈ X an is the limit of a net\n(vi ) in X div such that vi ≤ v for all i. As any ϕ ∈ PSH(θ) is decreasing and usc, it follows\nthat ϕ(vi ) → ϕ(v), which provides a slightly more precise version of Corollary 4.23 in that\ncase.\n     We record another useful consequence of Lemma 4.26.\nProposition 4.28. For any ψ ∈ C0 (X), ϕ 7→ supX an (ϕ − ψ) is continuous on PSH(θ).\nProof. When ψ is PL, this is a direct consequence of Lemma 4.26. As above, the general\ncase follows by density of PL(X) in C0 (X) with respect to uniform convergence.     \u0003\nCorollary 4.29. For any v ∈ X an , the evaluation map ϕ 7→ ϕ(v) is usc on PSH(θ).\n  In other words, each convergent net ϕi → ϕ in PSH(θ) satisfies ϕ ≥ lim supi ϕi pointwise\non X an .\nProof. Pick a convergent net ϕi → ϕ in PSH(θ). Since ϕ is usc and X an is compact, we have\nϕ = inf{ψ ∈ C0 (X) | ψ ≥ ϕ} pointwise. For each candidate ψ and ε > 0, Proposition 4.28\nimplies that ϕi ≤ ψ + ε on X an for all i large enough. Thus lim supi ϕi ≤ ψ + ε pointwise\non X an , and the result follows by taking the infimum over ψ and letting ε → 0.        \u0003",

      "literal": "5.2. The envelope property and unibranch varieties. In the rest of §4 we assume that\nX is irreducible unless stated otherwise. (See Remark 5.15.)\nDefinition 5.8. We say that a class θ ∈ N1 (X) has the envelope property if, for any\nbounded-above family (ϕi ) in PSH(θ), the usc upper envelope sup?i ϕi is θ-psh.\n   In the complex analytic case, it is a basic fact that psh functions on domains in Cn\nsatisfy the analogue of the envelope property. As a consequence, θ-psh functions satisfy the\nenvelope property for any projective complex manifold X and any closed (1, 1)-form θ. More\ngenerally, the latter property holds when X is unibranch (i.e. locally analytically irreducible),\ncf. [Dem85, Théorème 1.7], but fails in general, for the same reason as Theorem 5.12 below.\n   With our global definition of θ-psh functions, the envelope property turns out to be\nespecially delicate. The purpose of what follows is to explore various implications, and\nestablish it in an important special case. We first observe:\nLemma 5.9. Assume that θ ∈ N1 (X) can be written as the limit of classes θj ≥ θ that\nsatisfy the envelope property. Then θ has the envelope property as well.\n\f58                       SÉBASTIEN BOUCKSOM AND MATTIAS JONSSON\n\nProof. Pick a bounded-above family (ϕi ) in PSH(θ). Since θ ≤ θj , we have PSH(θ) ⊂\nPSH(θj ). Thus sup?i ϕi is θj -psh for all j, and hence θ-psh, by Theorem 4.5.    \u0003\nCorollary 5.10. If the continuity property holds for all ω ∈ Amp(X), then it holds for all\nθ ∈ Nef(X).\n  We next prove that the envelope property is equivalent to the analogue of a basic com-\npactness property in the complex analytic case.\nTheorem 5.11. For any θ ∈ N1 (X), the following properties are equivalent:\n    (i) the envelope property holds for θ;\n   (ii) the space PSHsup (θ) := {ϕ ∈ PSH(θ) | sup ϕ = 0} is compact;\n  (iii) every bounded-above, increasing net (ϕj ) in PSH(θ) converges in PSH(θ).\nProof. Assume (i), and pick a net (ϕi ) in PSHsup (θ). By Proposition 4.38, (ϕi (v)) is a\nbounded net in R for each v ∈ X div . By Tychonoff’s theorem, after passing to a subnet,\nwe may thus assume that (ϕi ) converges pointwise on X div to a function ϕ : X div → R,\nand it suffices to show that ϕ extends to a function in PSH(θ) (necessarily unique, by\nCorollary 4.23). By (i), ψi := sup?j≥i ϕj is θ-psh for each i. The net (ψi ) is further decreasing,\nand its pointwise limit ψ := limi ψi is thus either θ-psh, or identically −∞, by Theorem 4.5.\nNow Theorem 5.6 implies that ψi = supj≥i ϕj on X div . Thus ψ = ϕ on X div . In particular,\nψ 6≡ −∞, hence ψ ∈ PSH(θ). This proves (i)⇒(ii).\n   Next, assume (ii), and consider a bounded-above, increasing net (ϕj ) in PSH(θ). Then a\nsubnet ϕji −sup ϕji converges to some ϕ ∈ PSH(θ). Since (sup ϕj ) is increasing and bounded-\nabove, it converges to some c ∈ R. Thus ϕji → ϕ + c on X div , and hence ϕj → ϕ + c on\nX div since (ϕj ) is increasing. This proves (ii)⇒(iii).\n   Finally, assume (iii). Let (ϕi )i∈I be a bounded-above family of θ-psh functions, and\nconsider the increasing net ψJ := maxi∈J ϕi parametrized by finite subsets J ⊂ I. Then ψJ\nadmits a limit ψ in PSH(θ), and we claim that ψ = sup?i ϕi . On X div we have\n                              ψ = lim max ϕi = supi ϕi = sup?i ϕi ,\n                                    J   i∈J\nby Theorem 5.6. Since ψ is θ-psh and sup?i ϕi is usc, Theorem 4.22 yields ψ ≤ sup?i ϕi on\nX an . Similarly, Theorem 4.22 yields ϕi ≤ ψ, and hence sup?i ϕi ≤ ψ, since ψ is usc. This\nproves the claim, and hence (iii)⇒(i).                                                  \u0003\n   Recall that the variety X is said to be unibranch if the following equivalent conditions\nhold (see [EGA, IV.7.6.3] and [Kol16, Corollary 32]):",

      "literal": "Theorem 5.20. Assume that X is smooth and connected, and that either char k = 0 or\ndim X ≤ 2. Then any θ ∈ Nef(X) has the envelope property.\n  We do not know whether a class θ ∈ N1 (X) that is not nef has the envelope property.\nCorollary 5.21. Under the assumptions of Theorem 5.20, the set\n                           PSHsup (θ) = {ϕ ∈ PSH(θ) | sup ϕ = 0}\nis compact.\n  In [BFJ16], the above compactness property was established in the discretely valued\ncase, by relying on much more involved arguments based on dual complexes and toroidal\nmodifications.\n\f62                      SÉBASTIEN BOUCKSOM AND MATTIAS JONSSON\n\nLemma 5.22. Let L be an ample line bundle on X, and (X , L) a test configuration for\n(X, L), with L an honest line bundle. Denote by am ⊂ OX the base ideal of mL, which is a\nvertical ideal of X for all m \u001d 1. Then\n                       PL (ϕL ) − ϕL = sup m−1 ϕam = lim m−1 ϕam\n                                       m≥1             m→∞\n\npointwise on X an . Furthermore, PL (ϕL ) is continuous iff m−1 ϕam converges uniformly on\nX an .\n  As with flag ideals, see §2.1, the function ϕam ∈ PL(X) is defined by ϕam (v) = −σ(v)(am ),\nwhere σ = σX denotes Gauss extension (see Remark 1.9).\nProof. Set ϕm := ϕL + m−1 ϕam . Note first that (am )m is a graded sequence of ideals, i.e.\nam · am0 ⊂ am+m0 for all m, m0 ∈ N. The sequence m 7→ ϕam is thus superadditive, and\nFekete’s lemma yields supm≥1 m−1 ϕam = limm→∞ m−1 ϕam .\n  Next write L = LX + D with a vertical Cartier divisor D on X , so that ϕL = ϕD . For\neach m, denote by µm : Xm → X the integral closure of the blowup along am , and by Dm the\n(antieffective) divisor on Xm such that OXm · am = OXm (Dm ); thus ϕm = ϕD + m−1 ϕDm .\nBy construction, OX (mL) ⊗ am is globally generated. This implies that\n                         µ? (mL) + Dm = mLXm + µ? (mD) + Dm\nis nef, and hence ϕm ∈ PL ∩ PSH(L). Furthermore, ϕDm ≤ 0, so ϕm ≤ ϕL , and hence\nϕm ≤ PL (ϕL ), which proves\n                               sup ϕm = lim ϕm ≤ PL (ϕL ).\n                               m≥1       m→∞\n\nConversely, Lemma 5.19 implies\n                      PL (ϕL ) = QL (ϕL ) = sup {ψ ∈ H(L) | ψ ≤ ϕL } .\nPick ψ ∈ H(L) with ψ ≤ ϕL . After replacing X with a higher test configuration, we may\nand do assume, for the sake of notational simplicity, that ψ is determined by a vertical\nQ-Cartier divisor E on X . For m large and divisible enough, Theorem 2.31 shows that\nmLX + mE = mL + m(E − D) is globally generated, and hence OX (m(E − D)) ⊂ am . This\nyields\n                             m(ψ − ϕL ) = ϕm(E−D) ≤ ϕam ,\nand hence ψ ≤ ϕm , which yields, as desired, the converse inequality\n                      PL (ϕL ) = sup {ψ ∈ H(L) | ψ ≤ ϕL } ≤ sup ϕm .\n                                                               m\nThe final assertion is a simple consequence of Dini’s lemma, using the superadditivity of\nm 7→ mϕm .                                                                             \u0003\nProof of Theorem 5.20. By Lemma 5.9, we may assume that θ = c1 (L) with L ∈ Pic(X)Q\nample. Pick ϕ ∈ C0 (X). By Lemma 5.17, we need to show that PL (ϕ) is continuous. Since\nPL(X) is dense in C0 (X) with respect to uniform convergence (see Theorem 2.2), we may\nassume ϕ ∈ PL(X), by (5.1). By Theorem 2.31, we have ϕ = ϕL for a test configuration\n(X , L) for (X, L). After replacing L with a multiple, we may further assume that L and L\nare honest line bundle.\n   Using the notation of Lemma 5.22, we need to show that ϕm := ϕL + m−1 ϕam converges\nuniformly to PL (ϕL ). Since we assume that char k = 0 or dim X ≤ 2 (and hence dim X ≤ 3),\nwe can rely on resolution of singularities and assume that X is smooth and X0 has simple\n\f             GLOBAL PLURIPOTENTIAL THEORY OVER A TRIVIALLY VALUED FIELD                         63\n\nnormal crossings support. We may also assume that there exists an effective vertical Q-\ndivisor E on X such that A := L − E is ample.\n   Assume first that char k = 0, and let bm be the multiplier ideal of the graded sequence am\n                                                                                            • .\n                                                            l\nThe inclusion am ⊂ bm is elementary, and we have bml ⊂ bm for all m, l by the subadditivity\nof multiplier ideals. This implies that\n                      m−1 ϕbm ≥ (ml)−1 ϕbml ≥ (ml)−1 ϕaml ≥ m−1 ϕam\nfor all sufficiently divisible m and l. Letting l → ∞ shows that\n                                ϕL + m−1 ϕbm ≥ PL (ϕL ) ≥ ϕm                                (5.2)\nfor all sufficiently divisible m. By the uniform global generation of multiplier ideals there\nexists m0 ∈ N such that\n\n               OX (mL + m0 A) ⊗ bm = OX ((m + m0 )L) ⊗ OX (−m0 E) ⊗ bm\n  is globally generated for all sufficiently divisible m. This implies that\n                                      ϕbm ≤ ϕam+m0 + C\nfor a constant C independent of m. Combining this with (5.2) yields\n\n                                            m0            m0        C\n                       ϕm ≤ PL (ϕL ) ≤ (1 +    )ϕm+m0 −       ϕL + ,\n                                            m              m        m\nfor all sufficiently divisible m. This shows that PL (ϕL ) is a uniform limit of continuous\nfunctions, and hence continuous.\n   When char k > 0, the very same argument applies with test ideals in place of multiplier\nideals, see [GJKM17] for details.                                                        \u0003\nRemark 5.23.       "literal": "12.1. The strong topology of E 1 . Following [BBGZ13, BBE+16] we introduce:\nDefinition 12.1. The weak topology of E 1 is the topology inherited from PSH(ω). The\nstrong topology is the coarsest refinement of the weak topology for which E : E 1 → R becomes\ncontinuous.\n  Thus a net (ϕj ) in E 1 converges weakly to ϕ ∈ E 1 iff ϕj → ϕ pointwise on X div (or,\nequivalently, on X lin , cf. Remark 11.5); it converges strongly iff we further have E(ϕj ) →\nE(ϕ). By Proposition 7.7, E is continuous along decreasing nets, and hence:\nExample 12.2. For a decreasing net (ϕi ) in E 1 , weak and strong convergence coincide.\nDefinition 12.3. We define the quasi-metric I on E 1 by setting\n                       I(ϕ, ϕ0 ) = Iω (ϕ, ϕ0 ) := I(ϕ, ϕ0 ) + | sup ϕ − sup ϕ0 |         (12.1)\nfor ϕ, ϕ0 ∈ E 1 .\n  By (7.30) and Corollary 10.4, I is indeed a quasi-metric. It is further immediate to check\nthat it satisfies the analogue of (7.34), i.e.\n                         I(ϕ, ψ) = I (ϕ, max{ϕ, ψ}) + I (max{ϕ, ψ}, ψ)                   (12.2)\nfor all ϕ, ψ ∈ E 1 . As we now show, the quasi-metric I defines the strong topology of E 1 .\nTheorem 12.4. A net (ϕj ) in E 1 converges strongly to ϕ ∈ E 1 iff I(ϕj , ϕ) → 0.\n\f             GLOBAL PLURIPOTENTIAL THEORY OVER A TRIVIALLY VALUED FIELD                      121\n\nProof. First assume that ϕj → ϕ strongly in E 1 . Then sup ϕj → sup ϕ, E(ϕj ) → E(ϕ), and\n       ´ sup ϕj − E(ϕj ) is thus eventually bounded. Since ϕj → ϕ weakly, Proposition 9.19\nJ(ϕj ) =\nyields (ϕj − ϕ) MA(ϕ) → 0. Thus\n                                                       ˆ\n                 I(ϕj , ϕ) ≈ Jϕ (ϕj ) = E(ϕ) − E(ϕj ) + (ϕj − ϕ) MA(ϕ) → 0,         (12.3)\n\nin view of (7.29), and hence I(ϕj , ϕ) → 0.\n   Assume, conversely, I(ϕj , ϕ) → 0, i.e. sup ϕj → sup ϕ and I(ϕj , ϕ) → 0. Since δv has finite\nenergy for v ∈ X div (Proposition 11.1), Theorem 10.3 yields ϕj (v) → ϕ(v), i.e. ϕj → ϕ\nweakly. By the quasi-triangle inequality,´ J(ϕj ) ≈ I(ϕ ´ j , 0) is further eventually bounded,\nand Proposition 9.19 therefore implies ϕj MA(ϕ) → ϕ MA(ϕ), which in turn shows that\nE(ϕj ) → E(ϕ), by (12.3). Thus ϕj → ϕ strongly, which concludes the proof.                    \u0003\n   By Example 12.2, any weakly convergent decreasing net in E 1 is strongly convergent. The\nnext result, which is a direct consequence of Theorem 7.38, shows that this also holds for\nincreasing nets:\nTheorem 12.5. Let (ϕj ) be an increasing net in E 1 , and assume that ϕj → ϕ weakly in\nE 1 .
