Let D be the rational linear span of differences of rational-weight primary FS functions at ξ0 on K, allowing positive rational equivariant slopes in intermediate representatives. Products after clearing exponents represent positive sums of logarithmic branches; maxima concatenate primary lists, and padding by the zero-on-K reference matches slopes. The uniform closure is a real vector lattice containing constants and separating points, hence is C(K) by lattice Stone–Weierstrass. Their slope is A, difference is f_η, and restriction difference is the single fixed f on K for every η. For rational η, expanding the reference maximum gives positive rational logarithmic coefficients. Clearing their denominators yields regular monomial sections with degree exactly the branch denominator for η/A. Their list is primary since off the vertex some original primary element and some reference generator are both nonzero. Thus a_η,b_η are actual FS(η/A). For irrational η, rational approximations and roots back to fixed η/A converge uniformly on K, proving actual CPSH membership. On the rational quotient their auxiliary class is Aω_η, not ω_η; its mixed-volume ratio is exactly (1+εA)^p.\n\nThe rational competitor correspondence requires an actual torification argument. L_d has its natural T-linearization. Choose a degree-d eigencharacter γ and twist by −γ, so the effective central Gm acts trivially and the linearization descends to the residual torus. Degree-m section weights become α−mγ. Eigen-section charts have degree-zero residual grading; taking component minima defines a continuous projective torification q_V, compatible under eigenunit localization. The canonical reference is fixed. Degree-zero ratios determine the base point, and the radial Gauss coordinate and residue weights identify its torified image with K, including kernels. Splitting every section of a projective FS metric into residual components, retaining its coefficient, gives the actual globally generating FS metric ψ∘q_V≥ψ by the ultrametric inequality. Frame transitions preserve it, and uniform limits give the CPSH assertion. If ψ≤g=g∘q_V, then ψ∘q_V≤g as well. Energy monotonicity equates torified and unrestricted energy suprema. Conversely eigen-section metrics lift to actual cone metrics through the torsor and finite cover. Therefore n2's obstacle identity applies to the genuine rational cone supremum, with all scalars in n1.\n\nFor any η define F_η(g)=sup{E_η(w):w∈CPSH(η),w|K≤g}. It is finite between min g and max g, monotone, exactly translation equivariant, and 1-Lipschitz. It agrees with existing energy on CPSH restrictions. Rationally it is precisely normalized relative volume on ω_η, relative to the canonical reference, by the preceding competitor construction and n2.\n\nBGM2004.03847v1 Lemma3.2 has the exact hypotheses of a geometrically reduced projective scheme over an arbitrary complete NA field, an ample L with continuous psh φ, and continuous psh ψ1,ψ2 on auxiliary ample M. For f=ψ1−ψ2 and C=(L+M)^p−L^p it proves C inf f ≤ ∫f(ddcφ+ddcψ1)^p−vol(L,φ+f,φ) ≤ C sup f, without an envelope antecedent. Our normal integral quotients meet its scheme hypothesis. Its proof passes to an algebraically closed nontrivially valued extension (BE9.12/8.3), uses uniform nef-model approximations and a higher reduced-special-fiber model, perturbs toward ample models and clears tensor powers. BE A.15's relatively regular sections apply to flat projective finitely presented relatively ample models, by prime avoidance at associated fiber points. BGM1.2 allows regular restriction to vertical divisors. Normalizing inf f=0 makes its divisor E effective on the reduced fiber; restriction sequences bound the determinant difference by Σ_(j=1)^m h0(E,(mL+jE)|E), at most m h0(E,m(L+M1)|E). BGM2.4 gives the latter content m^p/p! times its curvature integral plus O(m^(p−1)), using Serre vanishing and the degree-p first difference of the degree-(p+1) determinant pairing. Dividing by m^(p+1)/p! yields one side. Normalize sup f=0, use effective −E and the mL−j(−E) sequences for the other. Constant translation adds aC. This retains every factorial and intersection factor. BE9.15's continuous-psh equality is separate; its envelope-conditional Corollary9.16 is not used.\n\nApply the lemma to εψ1,εψ2 on εAω_η, first rational ε by tensor clearing and then real ε by uniform continuity. Divide by V=ω_η^p. The expanded positive mixed measure equals the original probability plus positive terms of total mass (1+εA)^p−1. Both the lemma's error and the added integral are bounded by ε times that mass times ||f||. For both signs (interchange ψ1,ψ2 when t<0), the genuine cone result is |F_η(u+tf)−E_η(u)−t∫_Kf MA_η(u)| ≤2|t|[(1+A|t|)^p−1]||f|| ≤C_(Q,f,τ)t², where C_(Q,f,τ)=2pA(1+Aτ)^(p−1)||f|| and |t|≤τ. For p=0 the remainder is zero. This holds for bounded rational-field potentials uniformly in the potential and denominators.\n\nHere is the bounded-obstacle passage in the field. If ||g||≤M, truncate every competitor below by max(w,ρ_ξ−M), retaining feasibility and increasing its energy. Now −M≤w|K≤M. Write w=Nw+a, |a|≤M, −2M≤Nw≤0. The normalized energy comparison and constant-shift root bound in n1 give |E_η(Rw)−E_ξ(w)|≤2M(e^((2n+1)δ)−1)+M(e^δ−1). The root's constraint error is ≤M(e^δ−1), so subtract that constant to make it feasible. Reverse the fields to obtain |F_η(g)−F_ξ(g)|≤2M[e^((2n+1)δ)−1+e^δ−1]. Rational raw root images of a fixed irrational-field CPSH potential converge locally uniformly. This obstacle modulus, 1-Lipschitzness and n1's actual bounded MA/energy convergence pass the same-direction quadratic estimate to irrational fields. Only bounded moving-field limits are used.\n\nThe fixed-field E1 limit can be checked without the printed index or density shortcuts in Wu. For u0,…,up≤0 in CPSH put bar u=(Σu_i)/n, B=max_i(−E(u_i)). Concavity gives E(bar u)≥−B. All energy terms are nonpositive. Retain the top term, use bar u≤u0/n, and retain the distinct u1,…,up term of the positive mixed expansion: E(bar u)≤(1/n)∫bar u MA(bar u)≤(1/n²)∫u0 MA(bar u)≤p!/n^(n+1)∫u0 MA(u1,…,up). Thus ∫u0 MA(u1,…,up)≥−n^(n+1)B/p!. This corrects the missing 1/n, the endpoint p and J(u)=u(v_triv)−E(u) in the source.\n\nFor a fixed Radon measure on compact Hausdorff K, integrals of a decreasing bounded-above usc net converge to the integral of its limit. Radon regularity identifies the latter as an infimum over continuous majorants; compactness of the increasing open sets {u_i<g+ε} gives the actual uncountable-net argument.\n\nInduct on the number r of E1 slots in a mixed measure, including joint decreasing continuity with an E1 weight. For r=0, the coarse bound and fixed-measure net integration give weight integrability and continuity. For r>0, integration by parts for a CPSH test ψ prescribes ∫ψ μ(u1,…,u_r,…) = ∫u_r μ(u1,…,ψ,…) + ∫(ψ−u_r) μ(u1,…,0,…). The right-hand measures have ≤r−1 E1 slots; their integrals converge by induction along actual raw defining nets. Approximating left sides are probabilities, so weak compactness supplies positive mass-one cluster limits. Differences of CPSH tests are dense by the direction construction above, hence the prescription uniquely specifies every cluster limit. This gives symmetry, independence of nets and weak decreasing continuity. Passing the coarse bound first with a fixed CPSH approximation of the weight, then decreasing that weight, proves its integrability for the new slot count. A common tail shift makes all potentials nonpositive and their finite energy bounds supply B; normalized approximants need not decrease.\n\nFor weighted continuity, telescoping the prescription, first for a CPSH weight and then by fixed-measure decreasing integration for an E1 weight ψ, gives for u_i'≥u_i:\n∫ψ μ(u1',…,u_r',…)≥∫ψ μ(u1,…,u_r,…)+Σ_(i=1)^r∫(u_i−u_i') μ(u1,…,u_(i−1),0,u_(i+1)',…,u_r',…).\nThe omitted terms integrate nonnegative differences against positive measures. If ψ_j,u_i,j decrease to ψ,u_i, weak convergence and continuous majorants give the usc upper bound. This lower inequality gives the opposite bound: every error tends to zero by the induction at r−1, once with fixed weight u_i and once with decreasing weight u_i,j. Hence weighted integrals converge. After a common shift, −ψ_jμ_j are positive measures with convergent masses; every cluster limit dominates −ψμ by lower semicontinuity against positive continuous tests, and equal masses force equality. # TASK-GRANTED exact accepted round73 partial mathematical binding P13.2-argument.md

# Exact scope and disposition

The unchanged original P13.2 is **not solved in this paper**. No intrinsic sufficiency or actual inverse existence is claimed in general dimension. The domain remains every normal good polarized affine cone over every algebraically closed trivially valued characteristic-zero field, with every Reeb field, including irrational fields. The carrier remains the fixed compact space $K=X_0^T$, and every MA below is the genuine affine-cone probability. The final flag is `candidate=false`.

The supplied full P13.1 mathematical proof, explicitly accepted with its original PASS review, is the sole accepted new theorem. The supplied round71, round70, and root79 drafts are not premises. This paper independently derives the finite-target and approximation assertions from the accepted calculus, and adds a linear fixed-ray homogeneous polynomial and a uniform bounded-energy target-integration estimate. It also reconstructs explicit strict supports, rational affine approximations at logarithmic poles, a normal-ring localization lemma, and a retraction argument for carrier envelopes. Those auxiliaries do not establish the normal singular rational envelope base.

The precise new estimate is as follows. Put $p=\dim X-1\ge1$ and $\alpha=2^{-p}$. For a Radon probability $\mu$ on $K$, define the finite-test dual energy
$$
 L(\mu)=\sup_{w\in H(\xi)}\left(E(w)-\int_Kw\,d\mu\right).
 \tag{1}
$$
For $L(\mu)<\infty$, let $\psi$ be an actual normalized finite-energy potential, with deficit
$$d_\psi=L(\mu)-E(\psi)+\int_K\psi\,d\mu.$$
Constants denoted $C_p$ depend only on $p$ and may be enlarged between displays. In particular,, for every actual normalized $\phi\in\mathcal E^1$,
$$
 \left|\int_K\phi\,d(\mathrm{MA}(\psi)-\mu)\right|
 \le C_p\sqrt{d_\psi}\,J(\phi)^\alpha
       \big(J(\phi)+J(\psi)+B_0\big)^{1/2-\alpha},
 \qquad B_0=1+\frac{L(\mu)+1}{2a_p},\quad a_p=\frac p{4^{p+1}}.
 \tag{2}
$$
Here $J(\phi)=-E(\phi)$ after normalization at the trivial valuation. If $p=1$, the last factor has exponent zero and is interpreted as one. Formula (2) proves uniform convergence of **signed target integrals** on bounded-$J$ sets along a maximizing sequence. It also proves convergence of the dual energies of its genuine output measures. It does not assert $L^1$ continuity along arbitrary weakly convergent nonmonotone potential nets, nor compactness or actual membership of a potential limit.

# Accepted calculus and exact normalization

Let $n=\dim X\ge1$, $p=n-1$, and write $\rho=\rho_\xi$, $V=V_\xi$. The actual continuous class is the local-uniform closure of finite primary homogeneous maxima
$$u=\max_i\frac{\lambda_i-v(f_i)}{\langle\xi,\mathrm{wt}(f_i)\rangle}.$$
PSH consists of nontrivial decreasing CPSH-net limits. Potentials in this paper are restrictions of those classes, not a replacement definition on $K$. The accepted theorem supplies genuine positive mixed products, integration by parts, the fixed-ray Hodge inequality, bounded local-uniform continuity, weighted decreasing-net continuity, and actual convexity and maximum stability.

The conventions are
$$
 \nu(u_1,\ldots,u_p)=V^{-1}dd^cu_1\cdots dd^cu_p\,dd^c\rho^+,
 \quad \rho^+=\max(\rho,0),\quad \nu(K)=1,
 \tag{3}
$$
$$
 E(u)=\frac1{nV}\sum_{j=0}^p\int_Ku\,(dd^cu)^j(dd^c\rho)^{p-j}dd^c\rho^+.
 \tag{4}
$$
The reference restricts to zero on $K$. On unbounded finite-energy potentials, these expressions mean the actual fixed-field weighted decreasing limits. The accepted outer energy is
$$F(g)=\sup_{w\in\mathrm{CPSH},\ w\le g}E(w),\qquad
 F^\downarrow(h)=\inf_{g\in C(K),\ g\ge h}F(g). \tag{5}$$
It agrees with the existing energy on every actual PSH potential and is decreasing-net continuous. For a fixed uniformly dense direction lattice $\mathcal T\subset C(K)$, each $f\in\mathcal T$ has actual slope-$A$ auxiliary representatives $f=a-b$ with $a/A,b/A\in\mathrm{CPSH}(\xi)$. Both signs of the accepted estimate are
$$
 |F^\downarrow(u+tf)-E(u)-t\int_Kf\,d\mathrm{MA}(u)|
 \le C_f t^2,\qquad C_f=2pA(1+A\tau)^{p-1}\|f\|_K,\quad |t|\le\tau.
 \tag{6}
$$
This is an outer functional, with no envelope-attainment identity assumed.

We also deduce the reference identity $\mathrm{MA}(\rho)=\delta_{v_{\rm triv}}$. Rationally, the zero-weight product model on the normal integral projective quotient has one central component of multiplicity one and mixed intersection equal to the projective volume. Its projective probability is the trivial-valuation Dirac mass. The accepted radial Gauss lift gives $v_{\rm triv}$ on $K$. Irrational references are bounded local-uniform limits of rational references; accepted genuine current continuity gives the same Dirac measure. Thus normalized $u$ has
$$u(v_{\rm triv})=\sup_Ku=0,\qquad u\le0,\qquad J(u)=-E(u). \tag{7}$$

For a rational field $\eta=s\eta_e$, the accepted effective supported grading has gcd one. On a suitable degree-$d$ Veronese quotient with actual ample bundle $L_d$, the calibration is
$$
 V_\eta=s^{-n}d^{1-n}(L_d^p),\quad \omega_\eta=L_d/(sd),\quad
 (\omega_\eta^p)=sV_\eta,\quad E_\eta=E_{L_d}/(sd). \tag{8}
$$
The finite map has generic degree $d$, the $n$ curvature factors each contribute $1/(sd)$, and the radial Gauss current has one unit mass. The full-ring signed mean has $E=(n+1)S/n$. We retain these factors throughout; there is no replacement by an unnormalized projective measure.

# Reconstruction of the finite-target criterion

For a decreasing bounded-above usc net $h_i\downarrow h$ on compact Hausdorff $K$ and a Radon probability $\mu$,
$$\int h_i\,d\mu\downarrow\int h\,d\mu. \tag{9}$$
Here the integral of a bounded-above usc function is the infimum of its continuous-majorant integrals. For bounded functions this follows by regular approximation of level sets; truncation below gives the extended version. If $g\ge h$ is continuous, the increasing open sets $\{h_i<g+\epsilon\}$ cover $K$. A finite subcover and directedness give $h_i\le g+\epsilon$ eventually. Taking the infimum over $g$ proves (9), including value $-\infty$. No sequence-only monotone convergence is substituted for nets.

Uniform $H$ approximation and actual energy continuity equate (1) with its CPSH supremum. If $L(\mu)<\infty$ and $u_i\downarrow u\in\mathcal E^1$ is an actual defining net, then
$$\int u_i\,d\mu\ge E(u_i)-L(\mu).$$
Equation (9) and decreasing energy continuity imply $\int u\,d\mu\ge E(u)-L(\mu)>-\infty$. All finite-energy potentials are therefore $\mu$-integrable, and
$$
 L(\mu)=\sup_{u\in\mathcal E^1}\left(E(u)-\int u\,d\mu\right)=E^\vee(\mu).
 \tag{10}
$$
When the bounded-test supremum is infinite, the E1 supremum is already infinite since it contains those bounded tests. This establishes equality as an extended functional as well.

Conversely, assume every actual E1 potential is $\mu$-integrable. On normalized $u$ put
$$b_l=-\int u\,d\nu(u^{[l]},\rho^{[p-l]})\ge0,\quad 0\le l\le p.$$
Weighted mixed continuity gives finiteness, the reference Dirac gives $b_0=0$, and $J(u)=n^{-1}\sum_l b_l$. The actual segment $u_t=tu+(1-t)\rho$, $0\le t\le1$, has on $K$ the restriction $tu$. Expanding the actual positive products gives
$$
 J(u_t)=\frac1n\sum_{l=1}^p
 t\sum_{j=l}^p{j\choose l}t^l(1-t)^{j-l}b_l
 \le2^nt^2J(u). \tag{11}
$$
Indeed $t^{l+1}\le t^2$ and $\sum_{j=l}^p{j\choose l}={n\choose l+1}\le2^n$. The expansion first holds for CPSH and passes through actual fixed-field weighted decreasing limits.

There is $B<\infty$ with $\int(-u)\,d\mu\le B$ on normalized $J(u)\le1$. Otherwise choose $u_j$ in that class with integrals at least $2^j$. The actual restrictions $w_m=\sum_{j\le m}2^{-j}u_j$, with the missing weight supplied by the reference off $K$, decrease, have value zero at $v_{\rm triv}$, and have energy at least $-1$ by concavity. Their actual nontrivial decreasing PSH limit is E1, but its negative integral is infinite. This contradicts the hypothesis. For $J(u)\ge2^{-n}$ take $t=(2^nJ(u))^{-1/2}$ in (11). For smaller $J$, use $B$. Thus
$$\int(-u)\,d\mu\le B+B\sqrt{2^nJ(u)},\qquad
 L(\mu)\le B+2^{n-2}B^2<\infty. \tag{12}$$
This independently reconstructs the finite-test integrability criterion on every original cone. It describes the finite-energy **target class**, without image sufficiency.

The accepted normalized root bijections are onto actual E1 classes and obey $u/c\le\mathcal R u\le u/C\le0$. Integrability of all source potentials consequently implies integrability of every target potential; the inverse gives the converse. Hence the finite-energy measure class is independent of Reeb field on the same $K$. No strong-topology assertion about roots is involved.

Fixed-ray concavity and first variation give the genuine tangent inequality
$$E(w)\le E(u)+\int(w-u)\,d\mathrm{MA}(u). \tag{13}$$
Raw simultaneous decreasing approximation and weighted mixed continuity extend it from continuous potentials to E1. Therefore every genuine output $\nu=\mathrm{MA}(u)$ has
$$L(\nu)=E(u)-\int u\,d\nu<\infty. \tag{14}$$
The printed opposite tangent sign in A, LF1249, is not used.

# Genuine approximation and midpoint Cauchyness

For continuous $g$ and every feasible $w\le g$ in (5),
$$E(w)-\int g\,d\mu\le E(w)-\int w\,d\mu\le L(\mu).$$
Thus $F(g)-\int g\,d\mu\le L(\mu)$. A decreasing net of continuous majorants of a bounded-above, integrable usc $h$ exists: minima make it directed, and separation of a point from a closed usc level set proves that its infimum is $h$. Equation (9) and outer decreasing continuity give
$$F^\downarrow(h)-\int h\,d\mu\le L(\mu). \tag{15}$$
Use (15) on $h=u+tf$ and the lower side of (6). Taking $t=s$ and $t=-s$ proves
$$
 \left|\int f\,d(\mathrm{MA}(u)-\mu)\right|\le d_u/s+C_fs,
 \qquad 0<s\le\tau. \tag{16}
$$
Choose actual $u_j\in H(\xi)$ with $d_j<j^{-2}$ and normalize by constants. For $s=1/j$, (16) gives error at most $(1+C_f)/j$ on $f\in\mathcal T$. Uniform approximation of any continuous test adds at most $2\|f-f_m\|$, since both measures are probabilities. Hence the **genuine** $\mathrm{MA}(u_j)$ converge weakly to $\mu$. The sequence comes from a real supremum, not an enumeration of all field elements. Deficit zero on an already genuine E1 potential gives the equation by both signs and density. This proves optimality of an actual maximizer, not attainment.

For $p\ge1$, put $z=u-v$ and, first for continuous same-field potentials,
$$ B_j=-V^{-1}\int z\,dd^cz\,(dd^cu)^j(dd^cv)^{p-1-j}dd^c\rho^+\ge0,
 \qquad 0\le j<p. \tag{17}$$
The accepted Hodge inequality supplies the sign. Difference of powers and integration by parts give
$$ I(u,v)=\int(u-v)\,d(\mathrm{MA}(v)-\mathrm{MA}(u))=\sum_{j=0}^{p-1}B_j. \tag{18}$$
For $g(t)=E(v+tz)$, genuine segment differentiation gives
$$-g''(t)=p\sum_{j=0}^{p-1}{p-1\choose j}t^j(1-t)^{p-1-j}B_j.$$
The midpoint Green kernel is $\frac12\min(t,1-t)$. On $[1/4,3/4]$ it is at least $1/8$, every monomial is at least $4^{-(p-1)}$, each binomial is at least one, and the interval length is $1/2$. Consequently
$$E((u+v)/2)-(E(u)+E(v))/2\ge a_p I(u,v),\quad a_p=p/4^{p+1}. \tag{19}$$
Each expression is a finite combination of accepted weighted mixed integrals. Actual raw decreasing CPSH approximants therefore pass the identity and inequality to E1. The actual midpoint deficit is nonnegative, giving
$$ I(u,v)\le(d_u+d_v)/(2a_p). \tag{20}$$
Every maximizing sequence is I-Cauchy modulo constants. Pairing normalized $u$ with the reference and using its deficit $L$ gives
$$ J(u)\le I(u,0)\le(d_u+L)/(2a_p). \tag{21}$$
The first inequality follows from the two nonnegative tangent deficits summing to $I$, with the reference deficit $J(u)$.

For completeness, a genuine-limit MA estimate follows directly from Hodge. Represent $f\in\mathcal T$ by slope-$A$ $a-b$. With $M_j=(dd^cu)^j(dd^cv)^{p-1-j}dd^c\rho^+$, Cauchy--Schwarz bounds the $j$th difference term by $\sqrt{Q_j(f)B_j}$, where
$$Q_j(f)=-V^{-1}\int f\,dd^cf\,M_j\le2A\|f\|_K.$$
Each positive product with $a$ or $b$ has mass $A$, by scaling the accepted mixed probability. Summing and using finite Cauchy--Schwarz gives
$$ |\int f\,d(\mathrm{MA}(u)-\mathrm{MA}(v))|
 \le\sqrt{2Ap\|f\|_K I(u,v)}. \tag{22}$$
Weighted decreasing continuity extends this to E1. Thus an actual E1 I-limit of the maximizing sequence would realize $\mu$. No such actual limit is obtained from (20).

# A genuinely linear fixed-ray polynomial

We now construct the finite linear algebra needed for the new bound; we do not apply the synthetic inverse theory.

Let $\mathcal D$ be the real vector space spanned by restrictions of actual CPSH potentials at this one fixed $\xi$, including constants. It is uniformly dense because it contains $\mathcal T$. Each $d\in\mathcal D$ has a unique zero-slope equivariant extension $\widetilde d(v)=d(s_\xi(v))$ to the torified punctured cone, then by torification to the punctured cone. It is a finite linear combination of genuine CPSH differences. Its distributional $dd^c$ is well defined, and all products below are finite linear combinations of the accepted bounded positive products. This uses one fixed linear class, not nonlinear root maps.

Put $\mathcal V=\mathbb R\times\mathcal D$ and project onto $\Theta=\mathbb R$ by the first coordinate. For $x=(s,d)$ set $\Omega_x=s\,dd^c\rho+dd^c\widetilde d$ and define the homogeneous polynomial of degree $p+1$
$$
 H(s,d)=\sum_{l=0}^p\frac{{p\choose l}}{l+1}s^{p-l}
 V^{-1}\int_K d\,(dd^c\widetilde d)^l(dd^c\rho)^{p-l}dd^c\rho^+,
 \qquad \mathcal F=-H. \tag{23}
$$
The primitive form is $H(s,d)=\int_0^1V^{-1}\int d(sdd^c\rho+tdd^c\widetilde d)^pdd^c\rho^+\,dt$. Expanding (4) and using $\sum_{j=l}^p{j\choose l}={p+1\choose l+1}$ proves $H(1,u)=E(u)$.

Integration by parts makes the $l+1$ differentiated copies of $d$ equal, hence for a zero-slope direction $f$,
$$\partial_d H(s,d)[f]=V^{-1}\int f\,\Omega_x^p dd^c\rho^+. \tag{24}$$
Polarization of this finite polynomial gives a symmetric $(p+1)$-linear pairing. For two zero-slope directions $a,b$ and $x_2,\ldots,x_p$,
$$
 \mathcal F\big((0,a),(0,b),x_2,\ldots,x_p\big)
 =-\frac1{p+1}V^{-1}\int a\,dd^c\widetilde b\,
        \Omega_{x_2}\cdots\Omega_{x_p}dd^c\rho^+. \tag{25}
$$
The coefficient follows by differentiating twice: $\partial^2\mathcal F=-p\int a\,dd^cb\,\Omega^{p-1}/V$, whereas a degree-$(p+1)$ polarization contributes $(p+1)p$.

Take the convex cone
$$\mathcal P=\{(s,d):s>0,\ d/s\in\mathrm{CPSH}(\xi)|_K\}
              \ \cup\ \{(0,c):c\in\mathbb R\}. \tag{26}$$
Positive combinations stay in it by accepted actual convexity and translation. The curvature of $(0,c)$ is zero. For all positive slots in (25), the fixed-ray Hodge index gives
$$a^2\cdot x_2\cdots x_p\ge0\qquad(a\in\mathcal V_0,\ x_i\in\mathcal P). \tag{27}$$
These are precisely the surjective linear projection, homogeneous polynomial, convex cone, and quadratic sign hypotheses of D Appendix A. No nice order on a global space $Z$, positive cohomology cone, ample-class identification, model axiom, topology of a generated PSH cone, or synthetic inverse theorem is inferred. They are not needed for this finite algebra application.

For $x=(1,u)$, $y=(1,v)$, its polynomial deficit is
$$\delta(x,y)=\mathcal F(x)-\mathcal F(y)-\langle\mathcal F'(y),x-y\rangle
             =E(v)-E(u)+\int(u-v)\,d\mathrm{MA}(v). \tag{28}$$
The nonnegative coefficients in its expansion give
$$I(u,v)/(p+1)\le\delta(x,y)\le pI(u,v)/(p+1),\quad
 \delta(x,y)+\delta(y,x)=I(u,v). \tag{29}$$
On normalized $u$, $\delta((1,u),(1,0))=J(u)$.

# Checking the polynomial Hölder estimate and its source application

We state the relevant consequence of D Theorem A.3, and check its proof mechanism so that the source use is not an analogy. For a fixed fiber, define
$$q(x,y)=\max_{0\le j<p}(x-y)^2y^jx^{p-1-j}.$$
The positive coefficient expansion and binomial theorem give $q\asymp\delta\asymp(x-y)^2((x+y)/2)^{p-1}$, with constants depending only on $p$.

Write $w=(x+y)/2$, $A=q(x,y)$ and $B=\max(q(x,z),q(y,z))$. For $b_j=(x-y)^2z^jw^{p-1-j}$, Cauchy--Schwarz and $2w-x=y\in\mathcal P$ give
$$b_0\le C_pA,\qquad b_{j+1}\le b_j+C_p\sqrt{b_jB},\qquad
 b_{p-1}\le C_pA^{2^{1-p}}B^{1-2^{1-p}}. \tag{30}$$
The last inequality follows by induction when $A\le B$; when $A\ge B$, the triangle inequality for the seminorm with positive slots gives $b_{p-1}\le4B$. To justify the bound used in the recurrence, convexity of $\delta(\cdot,z)$ gives $q(z,w)\le C_pB$. This convexity itself follows from the positive quadratic sign (27). Thus no quasi-triangle inequality has been assumed before its proof.

Using (30) on $x-z$ and $y-z$ with positive slot $w$ gives
$$q(x,y)\le C_pT^{2^{1-p}}\max(q(x,y),T)^{1-2^{1-p}},\quad
 T=\max(q(x,z),q(y,z)).$$
If $q(x,y)>T$, absorb its indicated power; otherwise the desired bound is already true. Hence $q(x,y)\le C'_pT$. Averaging finitely many positive slots and using this quasi-triangle inequality gives the general mixed estimate
$$|(x_0-y_0)(x_1-y_1)z_2\cdots z_p|
 \le C_p\delta(x_0,y_0)^\alpha\delta(x_1,y_1)^\alpha M^{1-2\alpha},
 \quad \alpha=2^{-p},\quad M=\max_\zeta\delta(\zeta,x_*). \tag{31}$$
For the equal-pair case this is (30), after bounding distances to the average of the $z_i$ by the reference distances; the unequal-pair case follows by Cauchy--Schwarz. Expanding $x_0^p-y_0^p$ and applying Cauchy--Schwarz once more, the first quadratic factor is comparable with $\delta(x_0,y_0)$ and (31) bounds the other. Thus
$$
 |\langle\mathcal F'(x_0)-\mathcal F'(y_0),x_1-y_1\rangle|
 \le C_p\delta(x_0,y_0)^{1/2}\delta(x_1,y_1)^\alpha M^{1/2-\alpha}.
 \tag{32}
$$
For $p=1$ this is the direct quadratic Cauchy--Schwarz inequality. These are the pertinent proof bodies of D Appendix A, LF2053--2235; (30) explicitly retains the square root in the recurrence. The source's implicit constants are not replaced by a falsely exact numerical constant.

With (24), (29), and reference $x_*=(1,0)$, (32) becomes the genuine cone bound
$$
 \left|\int(u-v)\,d(\mathrm{MA}(w)-\mathrm{MA}(z))\right|
 \le C_p I(w,z)^{1/2}I(u,v)^\alpha
       \max\{J(u),J(v),J(w),J(z)\}^{1/2-\alpha}
 \tag{33}
$$
for normalized actual continuous potentials. Constants disappear from the measure difference. Raw decreasing approximation extends (33) to E1: subtracting the decreasing values at $v_{\rm triv}$ need not give a decreasing net, but each weighted integral differs from its raw decreasing counterpart by just that convergent constant. Energies and $I$ therefore converge by accepted weighted continuity. All products and all limits have one fixed irrational or rational $\xi$. The constants depend only on $p$.

# Uniform target integrals and output dual energies

Choose normalized maximizing $w_k\in H$ with $d_k\to0$ and $d_k\le1$. Equation (21) gives $J(w_k)\le B_0$. In (33), take $u=\phi$, $v=0$, $w=\psi$, $z=w_k$. From (20) and (29),
$$
 |\int\phi\,d(\mathrm{MA}(\psi)-\mathrm{MA}(w_k))|
 \le C_p\sqrt{d_\psi+d_k}\,J(\phi)^\alpha
       (J(\phi)+J(\psi)+B_0)^{1/2-\alpha}. \tag{34}
$$
For continuous $\phi$, weak convergence from (16) lets $k\to\infty$, proving (2). The order of limits for unbounded $\phi$ is essential. First approximate it by its actual decreasing CPSH net, normalize each approximant, and prove the target estimate for each fixed approximant by sending $k\to\infty$. Then send the defining net to its limit. The fixed target uses (9)--(10); the fixed measure $\mathrm{MA}(\psi)$ uses accepted weighted continuity. The energies converge, and the subtracted normalization constants tend to zero. This proves (2) on all actual E1, with no unjustified passage of an unbounded varying test through weak measure convergence.

In particular, for $J(\phi)\le B$ and $d_j\le1$,
$$
 \sup_{J(\phi)\le B}\left|\int\phi\,d(\mathrm{MA}(w_j)-\mu)\right|
 \le C_p\sqrt{d_j}\,B^\alpha(B+2B_0)^{1/2-\alpha}. \tag{35}
$$
All tests are normalized. For $B=0$ the conclusion is obtained by the same zero-distance inequality, rather than dividing by zero. These are signed integrals. Neither (35) nor decreasing continuity alone proves absolute $L^1$ continuity along an arbitrary weak potential net.

Putting $\phi=w_j$ in (2), the genuine tangent identity (14) yields
$$
 |L(\mathrm{MA}(w_j))-L(\mu)|
 \le d_j+C_p\sqrt{d_j}\,B_0^{1/2}. \tag{36}
$$
Thus these outputs converge to $\mu$ weakly **and** in dual energy. This is an actual measure conclusion, not a potential realization.

Also take $w=w_j$, $z=0$ in (33) and then send $j\to\infty$, using the same continuous-test-first, decreasing-test-second order. For normalized $u,v\in\mathcal E^1$ this gives
$$
 |\int(u-v)\,d\mu|
 \le C_p I(u,v)^\alpha B_0^{1/2}
       (B_0+J(u)+J(v))^{1/2-\alpha}. \tag{37}
$$
Consequently the target integrals of a normalized maximizing sequence are Cauchy. Since $E(w_j)-\int w_j\,d\mu\to L(\mu)$, its normalized energies also converge to a finite value. This strengthens bounded energy, but still supplies no actual potential.

# Explicit strict supports with infinite coordinates

The following auxiliary reconstruction is independent of the unreviewed local/global bridge. On $\mathbb T^N=(\mathbb R\cup\{+\infty\})^N$, choose an integer $a\ge\log(N+1)$ and define
$$\chi_N(t)=a^{-1}\log\left(1+\sum_{i=1}^Ne^{-at_i}\right),\quad e^{-a\infty}=0. \tag{38}$$
It is continuous and convex on finite coordinates, decreases in each coordinate, and lies between $\max(0,-t_i)$ and that maximum plus one. At $t$, let $I$ be its finite coordinates and let $p_i=e^{-at_i}/(1+\sum_{i\in I}e^{-at_i})$. The real support
$$\ell_t(s)=\chi_N(t)-\sum_{i\in I}p_i(s_i-t_i) \tag{39}$$
has coefficient zero on every infinite coordinate. If a finite supported coordinate becomes infinite, its value is $-\infty$. The convex log-sum inequality gives $\chi_N\ge\ell_t$, with equality exactly at $s=t$: on the finite face strict convexity follows from the extra positive constant term, and making a formerly infinite coordinate finite increases $\chi_N$ strictly.

For every neighborhood $U$ of $t$, there is $\delta>0$ with $\chi_N-\ell_t\ge\delta$ outside $U$. Along a nonzero recession vector in the finite coordinates,
$$\max(0,-v_i:i\in I)+\sum_{i\in I}p_iv_i>0,$$
because every $p_i>0$ and $\sum p_i<1$. Thus a bounded difference sublevel confines the finite supported coordinates. It also bounds every other coordinate below, since a coordinate tending to $-\infty$ sends $\chi_N$ to $+\infty$. Its closure is compact in the extended product; at a supported coordinate $+\infty$ the difference tends to $+\infty$. The continuous difference on the remaining compact sublevel has the singleton zero set $\{t\}$. Compactness proves the gap, including the case $I$ empty.

Actual rational AFS approximation is explicit. Put $z_0=1$, $z_i=e^{-at_i}$ and $B_m={m+N\choose N}$. The multinomial expansion shows
$$
 q_m(t)=\max_{|\beta|=m}\left\{-\sum_{i=1}^N\frac{\beta_i}{m}t_i
          +\frac{\log{m\choose\beta}}{am}\right\}
 \le\chi_N(t)\le q_m(t)+\frac{\log B_m}{am}. \tag{40}
$$
This holds uniformly on the whole extended product. A term with positive $\beta_i$ at $t_i=\infty$ is $-\infty$; $\beta_0=m$ supplies a zero floor. Round intercepts upward rationally, with error at most $\epsilon_j/4$, and choose $m_j$ with the other error at most $\epsilon_j/4$. For rational $\epsilon_{j+1}\le\epsilon_j/4$, adding $\epsilon_j$ makes the resulting maxima decrease: the lower error bound at level $j$ exceeds the upper error bound at level $j+1$. They converge to $\chi_N$.

After $t_i=-\log|f_i|$, a branch is $m^{-1}\log|\prod f_i^{\beta_i}|$ plus a rational intercept. If the list includes the original affine algebra generators, the terms $\beta_i=1$, $\beta_0=m-1$ contain the generators themselves. Thus these are actual E-Definition-3.15 finite AFS presentations, with generators and floor retained. This proves the approximation directly and does not infer it from a singular envelope theorem.

Real logarithmic supports also need a pole-sensitive construction. Let $H\ge0$ be a rational AFS exhaustion in the original generators and choose integers $D_i\ge1$ with $\log|f_i|\le D_iH$ (enlarge $H$ by a constant). For finitely many strictly positive real $p_i$, put
$$ F_{ij}=\max(\log|f_i|,2^{-j}H-j),\qquad
 q_{ij}\uparrow p_i,\quad r_{ij}\downarrow p_i,
 \quad \varepsilon_j=\sum_iD_i(r_{ij}-q_{ij}),$$
where the bounds are rational and nested. Choose rational $C_j\downarrow C$. Then
$$L_j=\sum_iq_{ij}F_{ij}+(\varepsilon_j+2^{-j})H+C_j \tag{41}$$
are decreasing actual AFS and converge to $\sum_ip_i\log|f_i|+C$. They dominate that sum because $F_{ij}\ge\log|f_i|$ and $F_{ij}\le D_iH$. Monotonicity follows from
$$\varepsilon_j-\varepsilon_{j+1}\ge\sum_iD_i(q_{i,j+1}-q_{ij}),\quad
 F_{i,j+1}\le F_{ij}.$$
At a zero of an $f_i$ with $p_i>0$, its term tends to $-\infty$ while all other terms are bounded above at that point. Zero coefficients are omitted. To check the exact embedding definition, $L_j$ has a positive lower multiple of $H$ minus a finite constant, since $F_{ij}\ge2^{-j}H-j$. Each original generator can therefore be appended with a sufficiently low rational intercept without changing the maximum. Positive rational sums are finite maxima of logarithmic monomials. There is no unsupported uniform approximation claim at a logarithmic pole.

# A normal-ring localization estimate with actual divisor orders

Let $A$ be a normal integral finitely generated algebra over the original $k$, $f\ne0$, $h\in A_f$, and $U$ an actual global rational AFS. Suppose
$$b+a\log|h|\le\max(U,-c\log|f|)+C\quad\hbox{on }(\operatorname{Spec}A_f)^{\rm an},\quad a,c>0. \tag{42}$$
For a prime divisor $D$ of $\operatorname{Spec}A$, use the rays $r\operatorname{ord}_D$, $r>0$. Every regular generator of $U$ has nonnegative divisor order, so $U$ is bounded above there. Sending $r\to\infty$ in (42) gives
$$a\max(-\operatorname{ord}_Dh,0)\le c\operatorname{ord}_Df. \tag{43}$$
All orders are orders of the actual rational function. A chosen denominator exponent is not a divisor order: $f=z^2$, $h=z^{-1}$, $a=2$, $c=1$ has equality in (43), although a denominator representation can suggest the wrong bound.

For positive integers $r,q$ with $q/r>c/a$, $h^rf^q$ has no prime-divisor pole. Normality gives $h^rf^q\in A$: the height-one DVR intersection property identifies regular elements with those having nonnegative orders. This is the normal-domain Hartogs algebra already contained in the accepted rational bridge; no normalization hull replaces the original $A$ hull.

Let $x$ be in the $A$-polynomial hull of compact $L\subset (\operatorname{Spec}A_f)^{\rm an}$, and assume $|\log|f||\le M$ on $L\cup\{x\}$. Apply the hull inequality to $(h^rf^q)^l\in A$. Dividing by $rl$, or directly using its logarithm, gives
$$a\log|h(x)|+b\le\sup_L(a\log|h|+b)+2a(q/r)M.$$
Let $q/r\downarrow c/a$. Then
$$a\log|h(x)|+b\le\sup_L(a\log|h|+b)+2cM. \tag{44}$$
If $h(x)=0$ the assertion is immediate; if $h$ vanishes everywhere on $L$, the same polynomial forces $h(x)=0$. Hence no subtraction of infinities was used. This simplification is valid for normal $A$ and does not prove controlled AFS approximation of a weak input on $A_f$. Such approximation remains a separate premise before a localized-hull or Runge application.

# Carrier envelopes: the retraction issue and raw roots

We isolate a topological lemma which avoids an openness assumption for torification. Let $q:Y\to Y$ be a continuous retraction on a compact Hausdorff space with image $B$. For a bounded-above $U$ satisfying $U=U\circ q$,
$$U_Y^*|_B=(U|_B)_B^*. \tag{45}$$
For any neighborhood $N$ of $b\in B$ in $B$, the set $q^{-1}(N)$ is a neighborhood of $b$ in $Y$, and the supremum of $U$ on it is the supremum on $N$. This gives one limsup inequality. Neighborhoods in the ambient space restrict to neighborhoods in $B$, giving the other. No openness of $q$ is required.

At a rational Reeb field the accepted quotient gives residual torification $q_V$ and its compact image $B$ identified with $K$ by the calibrated Gauss lift. Splitting projective FS sections into residual weight components with their original coefficients produces an actual globally generating FS metric $\psi\circ q_V\ge\psi$. Uniform limits and actual decreasing limits preserve membership and composition. Thus if unrestricted projective EP were independently proved on this actual normal quotient, apply it to the invariant family, then torify its actual envelope. Equation (45) identifies its carrier restriction with the carrier usc supremum. This gives the invariant carrier property without any claim about a full-link envelope equality away from the image.

The raw-root transport can be checked independently. On torified points off the vertex, the accepted maps give $\Omega\simeq K\times\mathbb R$, with $v\mapsto(s_\xi(v),\rho_\xi(v))$ and inverse $(x,r)\mapsto x-r\xi$. An equivariant extension has usc regularization $U^*(v)=h^*(s_\xi(v))+\rho_\xi(v)$. For a uniformly upper-bounded family, its supremum $U$ and $U^*$ satisfy the same uniform orbit slopes $0<c\le C$:
$$U(v)-Ct\le U(v+t\eta)\le U(v)-ct\quad(t\ge0).$$
Every orbit is finite with a unique root or identically $-\infty$. Bracketing the zero shows
$$\mathcal R(\sup_i u_i)=\sup_i\mathcal R u_i,\qquad
 \mathcal R(U^*)=(\mathcal R U)^*. \tag{46}$$
For the second identity, a value above the root has negative $U^*$ on a neighborhood and pulls back to an upper root bound. A value below the root has every neighborhood meeting $\{U>0\}$ and pulls back to a lower limsup root bound. If the root is $-\infty$, the upper argument works for every real parameter. Applying the accepted actual raw inverse then transports carrier usc-sup membership between Reeb fields. This transfers a property **after** its rational normal base is proved; it transports neither $I$ completeness nor strong topology.

# What the singular source and the synthetic source leave unresolved

E is the exact public preprint 2609.27610v1. Its PDF and extract SHA256 hashes, respectively, are:


Its printed Definition 3.15 at LF766--768 is LOWER growth; Proposition 4.19 at LF1560--1562 asserts equality with global decreasing AFS regularizability. Its proof LF1569--1572 seeks an upper bound and invokes Lemma 3.25(1), whose LF952--957 antecedent already includes regularizability.

For completeness, the supplied counterexample is independently reconstructed. On $\mathbb A^1_k$, $v=(\max(\log|z|,0))^2$ is locally psh under the printed local definition: on $\{|z|<e^M\}$ with rational $M>0$, dyadic secants of $t^2$ on $[-M,0]$, $t=-\log|z|$, extended by zero for $t\ge0$, are convex rational PL and decrease to $v$. Near $t=\infty$ they are the constant zero, so their active affine branches are finite on their charts. The global AFS $g=\max(\log|z|,0)$ satisfies $g\le v+1/4$. On the Gauss ray $|z|=e^t$, every global AFS is bounded above by $At+B$ for finite $A,B$, since every polynomial has log norm $(\deg f)t$. A first decreasing approximant cannot majorize $t^2$ for all $t\ge0$. Thus the LOWER-growth proposition is false as printed. A corrected UPPER-growth proposition is not disproved by this example; it needs a new proof.

Our explicit supports (38)--(41) repair particular elementary barrier operations. They do not prove the following indispensable antecedents: an actual strict Laurent PL/formal representation for corrected local selections; analytic curve unit balancing; represented nef-metric detection; same-class BE cancellation and calibrated finite-Laurent Gauss coefficient descent; common collars and weighted local products; smooth-target descent, controlled removal, and determinant/radial trace construction; a single proper witness and whole winning collars in the split-piece weak maximum principle; localization of the positive integer trace without losing the hull neighborhood, including a separate $\gamma=1$ argument; weak Hartogs-domain Runge **before** coefficient radii; finite projection and reduced singular-stratum induction; and genuine countable rational normal EP.

The primary E proof of Theorem 7.1 at LF2704--2717 explicitly invokes weak/global Theorem 6.5. Hence its final normal EP statement is not an independent premise bypassing that chain. The printed integer trace inference at LF2451--2461 also does not prove one constant on a disconnected open. The supplied $z\mapsto z^2$ two-disk example demonstrates traces two and one on different components; a compatible local shrink and $\gamma=1$ treatment are necessary. We have not established them for the entire weak Runge argument.

Removing field cardinality is likewise not automatic. A valid route descends finite PL/reference data, verifies exact positive MA base change and contact orthogonality, and uses domination over larger countable fields. It need not descend every PSH competitor to one small field. That procedure requires the countable rational normal envelope base first. No such base is proved here.

D is the exact synthetic source 2307.01697v1. Its Theorem A, LF134--145, has conclusions about a dense image of **test functions in the space of measures**, a continuous quasimetric on that measure space, and its completeness. Remark 2.14 at LF1024--1029 explicitly sets aside pursuit of a generated PSH theory. Its theorem is therefore not an actual E1 realization theorem. We have verified only the Appendix A polynomial application (23)--(33). We have not supplied the common ordered $Z$, ample/model/direction axioms and generated-cone topology or actual cone identification needed for a larger synthetic application. Nonlinear roots are not such linear axioms.

The remaining original gap is actual realization: either construct an actual E1 I-limit with its membership proved, or complete the normal rational envelope base and then prove genuine normalized PSH compactness, weak bounded-$J$ target $L^1$ continuity, energy usc and variational attainment at every fixed irrational field. The signed estimates (35)--(37) improve target control but do not imply those missing geometric/topological claims. The weak limit of the genuine measures in (16) is not labeled an output without an actual potential.

For $n=1$, the accepted normal effective grading gives $R=k[z]$, $K=\{v_{\rm triv}\}$, $E(c)=c$, and the empty horizontal product with the radial unit current gives the sole probability $\delta_{v_{\rm triv}}$. That case is realized uniquely modulo constants. It does not supply any higher-dimensional realization.

All seventeen authorized source PDF/text hash sets, and supplied associated HTML files, are audited locally. Embedded drafts are retained as data in `embedded-data/`; none of their referenced campaign paths was opened. `status.json` has exactly `id,status,exact_result,proof_file,concrete_gaps`.

Lightweight exact checks cover fixed-ray coefficient identities, midpoint constants on finite dimensions, the dimension-one quadratic Hölder instance on a finite grid, dyadic counterexample secants, pole orders, generator terms and probability scalar cancellations. They are exhaustive only on their specified finite inputs. Hash matches, finite arithmetic and compilation certify neither universal mathematical correctness nor realization.

Existing Pandoc produces standalone TeX; existing pdfTeX compiles twice with separate retained logs. Programs, commands, environment versions and outputs are local. No installation, worker, other model, network request, private record, external write, source-cache edit or resource-heavy computation is used. No supervised compute facility is needed for the lightweight diagnostics.

This proves optimality of an actual maximizer, not attainment.\n\nFor $p\\ge1$, put $z=u-v$ and, first for continuous same-field potentials,\n$$ B_j=-V^{-1}\\int z\\,dd^cz\\,(dd^cu)^j(dd^cv)^{p-1-j}dd^c\\rho^+\\ge0,\n \\qquad 0\\le j<p. \\tag{17}$$\nThe accepted Hodge inequality supplies the sign. Difference of powers and integration by parts give\n$$ I(u,v)=\\int(u-v)\\,d(\\mathrm{MA}(v)-\\mathrm{MA}(u))=\\sum_{j=0}^{p-1}B_j. \\tag{18}$$\nFor $g(t)=E(v+tz)$, genuine segment differentiation gives\n$$-g''(t)=p\\sum_{j=0}^{p-1}{p-1\\choose j}t^j(1-t)^{p-1-j}B_j.$$\nThe midpoint Green kernel is $\\frac12\\min(t,1-t)$. On $[1/4,3/4]$ it is at least $1/8$, every monomial is at least $4^{-(p-1)}$, each binomial is at least one, and the interval length is $1/2$. Consequently\n$$E((u+v)/2)-(E(u)+E(v))/2\\ge a_p I(u,v),\\quad a_p=p/4^{p+1}. \\tag{19}$$\nEach expression is a finite combination of accepted weighted mixed integrals. Actual raw decreasing CPSH approximants therefore pass the identity and inequality to E1. The actual midpoint deficit is nonnegative, giving\n$$ I(u,v)\\le(d_u+d_v)/(2a_p). \\tag{20}$$\nEvery maximizing sequence is I-Cauchy modulo constants. Pairing normalized $u$ with the reference and using its deficit $L$ gives\n$$ J(u)\\le I(u,0)\\le(d_u+L)/(2a_p). \\tag{21}$$\nThe first inequality follows from the two nonnegative tangent deficits summing to $I$, with the reference deficit $J(u)$.\n\nFor completeness, a genuine-limit MA estimate follows directly from Hodge. Represent $f\\in\\mathcal T$ by slope-$A$ $a-b$. With $M_j=(dd^cu)^j(dd^cv)^{p-1-j}dd^c\\rho^+$, Cauchy--Schwarz bounds the $j$th difference term by $\\sqrt{Q_j(f)B_j}$, where\n$$Q_j(f)=-V^{-1}\\int f\\,dd^cf\\,M_j\\le2A\\|f\\|_K.$$\nEach positive product with $a$ or $b$ has mass $A$, by scaling the accepted mixed probability. Summing and using finite Cauchy--Schwarz gives\n$$ |\\int f\\,d(\\mathrm{MA}(u)-\\mathrm{MA}(v))|\n \\le\\sqrt{2Ap\\|f\\|_K I(u,v)}. \\tag{22}$$\nWeighted decreasing continuity extends this to E1. Thus an actual E1 I-limit of the maximizing sequence would realize $\\mu$. No such actual limit is obtained from (20).\n\n\n\nArtifacts in /workspace/research/campaigns/round-73/P13.2-public-source-normal-envelope-realization-and-fixed-ray-axioms/artifacts/codex-k8IJmq: P13.2-argument.md, P13.2-paper.tex, P13.2-paper.pdf; source-extracts/source-provenance.json retains the exact primary versions, hashes, literal proof bodies and inclusive LF coordinates.       "summary": "A genuinely linear fixed-ray polynomial yields uniform signed target-integration error O(sqrt(deficit)) on bounded-J sets and convergence of genuine output dual energies. It provides no actual inverse potential.",
      "detailedSummary": "At each fixed rational or irrational Reeb field, real spans of actual CPSH restrictions have unique zero-slope extensions. The degree-(p+1) primitive H(s,d) and cone of positive-slope actual CPSH pairs satisfy exactly Appendix A of 2307.01697v1; its polarization has the calibrated coefficient -1/(p+1), and Hodge supplies the quadratic sign. This justifies only the finite polynomial theorem, not a synthetic PSH or inverse theorem. The reconstructed Holder estimate has alpha=2^(-p). Midpoint Cauchyness, genuine weak approximation, and continuous-test-first/decreasing-test-second limits give uniform signed integration, dual-energy convergence, and convergence of maximizing-sequence target integrals and normalized energies. Absolute L1 continuity on arbitrary weak potential nets, actual membership, and realization remain unproved.",
      "text": "Use n1 for actual same-field products, normalization, target integrability, genuine approximate measures, midpoint bounds and weighted decreasing continuity. Here p=n-1>=1. Constants C_p depend only on p and may increase. The following linear construction and its source application are new reconstructed draft results; no nonlinear root is used as a linear axiom.\n\n# A genuinely linear fixed-ray polynomial\n\nWe now construct the finite linear algebra needed for the new bound; we do not apply the synthetic inverse theory.\n\nLet $\\mathcal D$ be the real vector space spanned by restrictions of actual CPSH potentials at this one fixed $\\xi$, including constants. It is uniformly dense because it contains $\\mathcal T$. Each $d\\in\\mathcal D$ has a unique zero-slope equivariant extension $\\widetilde d(v)=d(s_\\xi(v))$ to the torified punctured cone, then by torification to the punctured cone. It is a finite linear combination of genuine CPSH differences. Its distributional $dd^c$ is well defined, and all products below are finite linear combinations of the accepted bounded positive products. This uses one fixed linear class, not nonlinear root maps.\n\nPut $\\mathcal V=\\mathbb R\\times\\mathcal D$ and project onto $\\Theta=\\mathbb R$ by the first coordinate. For $x=(s,d)$ set $\\Omega_x=s\\,dd^c\\rho+dd^c\\widetilde d$ and define the homogeneous polynomial of degree $p+1$\n$$\n H(s,d)=\\sum_{l=0}^p\\frac{{p\\choose l}}{l+1}s^{p-l}\n V^{-1}\\int_K d\\,(dd^c\\widetilde d)^l(dd^c\\rho)^{p-l}dd^c\\rho^+,\n \\qquad \\mathcal F=-H. \\tag{23}\n$$\nThe primitive form is $H(s,d)=\\int_0^1V^{-1}\\int d(sdd^c\\rho+tdd^c\\widetilde d)^pdd^c\\rho^+\\,dt$. Expanding (4) and using $\\sum_{j=l}^p{j\\choose l}={p+1\\choose l+1}$ proves $H(1,u)=E(u)$.\n\nIntegration by parts makes the $l+1$ differentiated copies of $d$ equal, hence for a zero-slope direction $f$,\n$$\\partial_d H(s,d)[f]=V^{-1}\\int f\\,\\Omega_x^p dd^c\\rho^+. \\tag{24}$$\nPolarization of this finite polynomial gives a symmetric $(p+1)$-linear pairing. For two zero-slope directions $a,b$ and $x_2,\\ldots,x_p$,\n$$\n \\mathcal F\\big((0,a),(0,b),x_2,\\ldots,x_p\\big)\n =-\\frac1{p+1}V^{-1}\\int a\\,dd^c\\widetilde b\\,\n        \\Omega_{x_2}\\cdots\\Omega_{x_p}dd^c\\rho^+. \\tag{25}\n$$\nThe coefficient follows by differentiating twice: $\\partial^2\\mathcal F=-p\\int a\\,dd^cb\\,\\Omega^{p-1}/V$, whereas a degree-$(p+1)$ polarization contributes $(p+1)p$.\n\nTake the convex cone\n$$\\mathcal P=\\{(s,d):s>0,\\ d/s\\in\\mathrm{CPSH}(\\xi)|_K\\}\n              \\ \\cup\\ \\{(0,c):c\\in\\mathbb R\\}. \\tag{26}$$\nPositive combinations stay in it by accepted actual convexity and translation. The curvature of $(0,c)$ is zero. For all positive slots in (25), the fixed-ray Hodge index gives\n$$a^2\\cdot x_2\\cdots x_p\\ge0\\qquad(a\\in\\mathcal V_0,\\ x_i\\in\\mathcal P). \\tag{27}$$\nThese are precisely the surjective linear projection, homogeneous polynomial, convex cone, and quadratic sign hypotheses of D Appendix A. No nice order on a global space $Z$, positive cohomology cone, ample-class identification, model axiom, topology of a generated PSH cone, or synthetic inverse theorem is inferred. They are not needed for this finite algebra application.\n\nFor $x=(1,u)$, $y=(1,v)$, its polynomial deficit is\n$$\\delta(x,y)=\\mathcal F(x)-\\mathcal F(y)-\\langle\\mathcal F'(y),x-y\\rangle\n             =E(v)-E(u)+\\int(u-v)\\,d\\mathrm{MA}(v). \\tag{28}$$\nThe nonnegative coefficients in its expansion give\n$$I(u,v)/(p+1)\\le\\delta(x,y)\\le pI(u,v)/(p+1),\\quad\n \\delta(x,y)+\\delta(y,x)=I(u,v). \\tag{29}$$\nOn normalized $u$, $\\delta((1,u),(1,0))=J(u)$.\n\n# Checking the polynomial Hölder estimate and its source application\n\nWe state the relevant consequence of D Theorem A.3, and check its proof mechanism so that the source use is not an analogy. For a fixed fiber, define\n$$q(x,y)=\\max_{0\\le j<p}(x-y)^2y^jx^{p-1-j}.$$\nThe positive coefficient expansion and binomial theorem give $q\\asymp\\delta\\asymp(x-y)^2((x+y)/2)^{p-1}$, with constants depending only on $p$.\n\nWrite $w=(x+y)/2$, $A=q(x,y)$ and $B=\\max(q(x,z),q(y,z))$. For $b_j=(x-y)^2z^jw^{p-1-j}$, Cauchy--Schwarz and $2w-x=y\\in\\mathcal P$ give\n$$b_0\\le C_pA,\\qquad b_{j+1}\\le b_j+C_p\\sqrt{b_jB},\\qquad\n b_{p-1}\\le C_pA^{2^{1-p}}B^{1-2^{1-p}}. \\tag{30}$$\nThe last inequality follows by induction when $A\\le B$; when $A\\ge B$, the triangle inequality for the seminorm with positive slots gives $b_{p-1}\\le4B$. To justify the bound used in the recurrence, convexity of $\\delta(\\cdot,z)$ gives $q(z,w)\\le C_pB$. This convexity itself follows from the positive quadratic sign (27). Thus no quasi-triangle inequality has been assumed before its proof.\n\nUsing (30) on $x-z$ and $y-z$ with positive slot $w$ gives\n$$q(x,y)\\le C_pT^{2^{1-p}}\\max(q(x,y),T)^{1-2^{1-p}},\\quad\n T=\\max(q(x,z),q(y,z)).$$\nIf $q(x,y)>T$, absorb its indicated power; otherwise the desired bound is already true. Hence $q(x,y)\\le C'_pT$. Averaging finitely many positive slots and using this quasi-triangle inequality gives the general mixed estimate\n$$|(x_0-y_0)(x_1-y_1)z_2\\cdots z_p|\n \\le C_p\\delta(x_0,y_0)^\\alpha\\delta(x_1,y_1)^\\alpha M^{1-2\\alpha},\n \\quad \\alpha=2^{-p},\\quad M=\\max_\\zeta\\delta(\\zeta,x_*). \\tag{31}$$\nFor the equal-pair case this is (30), after bounding distances to the average of the $z_i$ by the reference distances; the unequal-pair case follows by Cauchy--Schwarz. Expanding $x_0^p-y_0^p$ and applying Cauchy--Schwarz once more, the first quadratic factor is comparable with $\\delta(x_0,y_0)$ and (31) bounds the other. Thus\n$$\n |\\langle\\mathcal F'(x_0)-\\mathcal F'(y_0),x_1-y_1\\rangle|\n \\le C_p\\delta(x_0,y_0)^{1/2}\\delta(x_1,y_1)^\\alpha M^{1/2-\\alpha}.\n \\tag{32}\n$$\nFor $p=1$ this is the direct quadratic Cauchy--Schwarz inequality. These are the pertinent proof bodies of D Appendix A, LF2053--2235; (30) explicitly retains the square root in the recurrence. The source's implicit constants are not replaced by a falsely exact numerical constant.\n\nWith (24), (29), and reference $x_*=(1,0)$, (32) becomes the genuine cone bound\n$$\n \\left|\\int(u-v)\\,d(\\mathrm{MA}(w)-\\mathrm{MA}(z))\\right|\n \\le C_p I(w,z)^{1/2}I(u,v)^\\alpha\n       \\max\\{J(u),J(v),J(w),J(z)\\}^{1/2-\\alpha}\n \\tag{33}\n$$\nfor normalized actual continuous potentials. Constants disappear from the measure difference. Raw decreasing approximation extends (33) to E1: subtracting the decreasing values at $v_{\\rm triv}$ need not give a decreasing net, but each weighted integral differs from its raw decreasing counterpart by just that convergent constant. Energies and $I$ therefore converge by accepted weighted continuity. All products and all limits have one fixed irrational or rational $\\xi$. The constants depend only on $p$.\n\n# Uniform target integrals and output dual energies\n\nChoose normalized maximizing $w_k\\in H$ with $d_k\\to0$ and $d_k\\le1$. Equation (21) gives $J(w_k)\\le B_0$. In (33), take $u=\\phi$, $v=0$, $w=\\psi$, $z=w_k$. From (20) and (29),\n$$\n |\\int\\phi\\,d(\\mathrm{MA}(\\psi)-\\mathrm{MA}(w_k))|\n \\le C_p\\sqrt{d_\\psi+d_k}\\,J(\\phi)^\\alpha\n       (J(\\phi)+J(\\psi)+B_0)^{1/2-\\alpha}. \\tag{34}\n$$\nFor continuous $\\phi$, weak convergence from (16) lets $k\\to\\infty$, proving (2). The order of limits for unbounded $\\phi$ is essential. First approximate it by its actual decreasing CPSH net, normalize each approximant, and prove the target estimate for each fixed approximant by sending $k\\to\\infty$. Then send the defining net to its limit. The fixed target uses (9)--(10); the fixed measure $\\mathrm{MA}(\\psi)$ uses accepted weighted continuity. The energies converge, and the subtracted normalization constants tend to zero. This proves (2) on all actual E1, with no unjustified passage of an unbounded varying test through weak measure convergence.\n\nIn particular, for $J(\\phi)\\le B$ and $d_j\\le1$,\n$$\n \\sup_{J(\\phi)\\le B}\\left|\\int\\phi\\,d(\\mathrm{MA}(w_j)-\\mu)\\right|\n \\le C_p\\sqrt{d_j}\\,B^\\alpha(B+2B_0)^{1/2-\\alpha}. \\tag{35}\n$$\nAll tests are normalized. For $B=0$ the conclusion is obtained by the same zero-distance inequality, rather than dividing by zero. These are signed integrals. Neither (35) nor decreasing continuity alone proves absolute $L^1$ continuity along an arbitrary weak potential net.\n\nPutting $\\phi=w_j$ in (2), the genuine tangent identity (14) yields\n$$\n |L(\\mathrm{MA}(w_j))-L(\\mu)|\n \\le d_j+C_p\\sqrt{d_j}\\,B_0^{1/2}. \\tag{36}\n$$\nThus these outputs converge to $\\mu$ weakly **and** in dual energy. This is an actual measure conclusion, not a potential realization.\n\nAlso take $w=w_j$, $z=0$ in (33) and then send $j\\to\\infty$, using the same continuous-test-first, decreasing-test-second order. For normalized $u,v\\in\\mathcal E^1$ this gives\n$$\n |\\int(u-v)\\,d\\mu|\n \\le C_p I(u,v)^\\alpha B_0^{1/2}\n       (B_0+J(u)+J(v))^{1/2-\\alpha}. \\tag{37}\n$$\nConsequently the target integrals of a normalized maximizing sequence are Cauchy. Since $E(w_j)-\\int w_j\\,d\\mu\\to L(\\mu)$, its normalized energies also converge to a finite value. This strengthens bounded energy, but still supplies no actual potential.\n\n\nThe primary D PDF SHA256 is 979daddbb2082320cd6bbe6f8522535e68b56d229317decd033c3101b9fdbaa1; its extract SHA256 is 8068829692eeb5ddcfd40de5a6141f415601c5b493e5578ad63e0dd3140bf2e4. The exact version is 2307.01697v1. Its pertinent complete Appendix A statement and proof bodies occupy inclusive LF2053--2235 under UTF-8 decode and split(\"\\n\"). The full synthetic Theorem A is not applied and does not conclude actual-potential existence.\n\nArtifacts in /workspace/research/campaigns/round-73/P13.2-public-source-normal-envelope-realization-and-fixed-ray-axioms/artifacts/codex-k8IJmq: P13.2-argument.md, P13.2-paper.tex, P13.2-paper.pdf; source-extracts/source-provenance.json retains the exact primary versions, hashes, literal proof bodies and inclusive LF coordinates.       "id": "n3",
      "summary": "Explicit strict logarithmic supports, pole-sensitive rational AFS approximation, normal divisor-order localization, and a retraction lemma for carrier envelopes are reconstructed. The normal weak Runge and envelope base still has indispensable gaps.",
      "detailedSummary": "Log-sum-exp has a unique real support contact and a positive gap outside every neighborhood, including infinite coordinates. The multinomial maximum has a global error bound and retains original generators; nested rational coefficients, local floors and a positive exhaustion term approximate real singular supports decreasingly. On normal affine rings, actual prime-divisor orders place h^r f^q in the original ring and give the original polynomial-hull inequality, avoiding denominator-counting errors. A continuous retraction identifies carrier usc regularizations without openness, and raw roots transport carrier membership only after a rational base exists. The printed lower-growth proposition is independently refuted, while corrected strict metric/curve/trace/Runge/finite-projection/singular induction and field descent remain unresolved. No final normal EP theorem is assumed.",
      "text": "Use n1 for the accepted rational quotient and residual torification, actual raw roots and fixed carrier conventions. Elementary barriers and the normal localization argument below are independent reconstructions of portions of the unreviewed root79 proposal. The round70 body and notes were documentary data, not supporting theorems.\n\n# Explicit strict supports with infinite coordinates\n\nThe following auxiliary reconstruction is independent of the unreviewed local/global bridge. On $\\mathbb T^N=(\\mathbb R\\cup\\{+\\infty\\})^N$, choose an integer $a\\ge\\log(N+1)$ and define\n$$\\chi_N(t)=a^{-1}\\log\\left(1+\\sum_{i=1}^Ne^{-at_i}\\right),\\quad e^{-a\\infty}=0. \\tag{38}$$\nIt is continuous and convex on finite coordinates, decreases in each coordinate, and lies between $\\max(0,-t_i)$ and that maximum plus one. At $t$, let $I$ be its finite coordinates and let $p_i=e^{-at_i}/(1+\\sum_{i\\in I}e^{-at_i})$. The real support\n$$\\ell_t(s)=\\chi_N(t)-\\sum_{i\\in I}p_i(s_i-t_i) \\tag{39}$$\nhas coefficient zero on every infinite coordinate. If a finite supported coordinate becomes infinite, its value is $-\\infty$. The convex log-sum inequality gives $\\chi_N\\ge\\ell_t$, with equality exactly at $s=t$: on the finite face strict convexity follows from the extra positive constant term, and making a formerly infinite coordinate finite increases $\\chi_N$ strictly.\n\nFor every neighborhood $U$ of $t$, there is $\\delta>0$ with $\\chi_N-\\ell_t\\ge\\delta$ outside $U$. Along a nonzero recession vector in the finite coordinates,\n$$\\max(0,-v_i:i\\in I)+\\sum_{i\\in I}p_iv_i>0,$$\nbecause every $p_i>0$ and $\\sum p_i<1$. Thus a bounded difference sublevel confines the finite supported coordinates. It also bounds every other coordinate below, since a coordinate tending to $-\\infty$ sends $\\chi_N$ to $+\\infty$. Its closure is compact in the extended product; at a supported coordinate $+\\infty$ the difference tends to $+\\infty$. The continuous difference on the remaining compact sublevel has the singleton zero set $\\{t\\}$. Compactness proves the gap, including the case $I$ empty.\n\nActual rational AFS approximation is explicit. Put $z_0=1$, $z_i=e^{-at_i}$ and $B_m={m+N\\choose N}$. The multinomial expansion shows\n$$\n q_m(t)=\\max_{|\\beta|=m}\\left\\{-\\sum_{i=1}^N\\frac{\\beta_i}{m}t_i\n          +\\frac{\\log{m\\choose\\beta}}{am}\\right\\}\n \\le\\chi_N(t)\\le q_m(t)+\\frac{\\log B_m}{am}. \\tag{40}\n$$\nThis holds uniformly on the whole extended product. A term with positive $\\beta_i$ at $t_i=\\infty$ is $-\\infty$; $\\beta_0=m$ supplies a zero floor. Round intercepts upward rationally, with error at most $\\epsilon_j/4$, and choose $m_j$ with the other error at most $\\epsilon_j/4$. For rational $\\epsilon_{j+1}\\le\\epsilon_j/4$, adding $\\epsilon_j$ makes the resulting maxima decrease: the lower error bound at level $j$ exceeds the upper error bound at level $j+1$. They converge to $\\chi_N$.\n\nAfter $t_i=-\\log|f_i|$, a branch is $m^{-1}\\log|\\prod f_i^{\\beta_i}|$ plus a rational intercept. If the list includes the original affine algebra generators, the terms $\\beta_i=1$, $\\beta_0=m-1$ contain the generators themselves. Thus these are actual E-Definition-3.15 finite AFS presentations, with generators and floor retained. This proves the approximation directly and does not infer it from a singular envelope theorem.\n\nReal logarithmic supports also need a pole-sensitive construction. Let $H\\ge0$ be a rational AFS exhaustion in the original generators and choose integers $D_i\\ge1$ with $\\log|f_i|\\le D_iH$ (enlarge $H$ by a constant). For finitely many strictly positive real $p_i$, put\n$$ F_{ij}=\\max(\\log|f_i|,2^{-j}H-j),\\qquad\n q_{ij}\\uparrow p_i,\\quad r_{ij}\\downarrow p_i,\n \\quad \\varepsilon_j=\\sum_iD_i(r_{ij}-q_{ij}),$$\nwhere the bounds are rational and nested. Choose rational $C_j\\downarrow C$. Then\n$$L_j=\\sum_iq_{ij}F_{ij}+(\\varepsilon_j+2^{-j})H+C_j \\tag{41}$$\nare decreasing actual AFS and converge to $\\sum_ip_i\\log|f_i|+C$. They dominate that sum because $F_{ij}\\ge\\log|f_i|$ and $F_{ij}\\le D_iH$. Monotonicity follows from\n$$\\varepsilon_j-\\varepsilon_{j+1}\\ge\\sum_iD_i(q_{i,j+1}-q_{ij}),\\quad\n F_{i,j+1}\\le F_{ij}.$$\nAt a zero of an $f_i$ with $p_i>0$, its term tends to $-\\infty$ while all other terms are bounded above at that point. Zero coefficients are omitted. To check the exact embedding definition, $L_j$ has a positive lower multiple of $H$ minus a finite constant, since $F_{ij}\\ge2^{-j}H-j$. Each original generator can therefore be appended with a sufficiently low rational intercept without changing the maximum. Positive rational sums are finite maxima of logarithmic monomials. There is no unsupported uniform approximation claim at a logarithmic pole.\n\n# A normal-ring localization estimate with actual divisor orders\n\nLet $A$ be a normal integral finitely generated algebra over the original $k$, $f\\ne0$, $h\\in A_f$, and $U$ an actual global rational AFS. Suppose\n$$b+a\\log|h|\\le\\max(U,-c\\log|f|)+C\\quad\\hbox{on }(\\operatorname{Spec}A_f)^{\\rm an},\\quad a,c>0. \\tag{42}$$\nFor a prime divisor $D$ of $\\operatorname{Spec}A$, use the rays $r\\operatorname{ord}_D$, $r>0$. Every regular generator of $U$ has nonnegative divisor order, so $U$ is bounded above there. Sending $r\\to\\infty$ in (42) gives\n$$a\\max(-\\operatorname{ord}_Dh,0)\\le c\\operatorname{ord}_Df. \\tag{43}$$\nAll orders are orders of the actual rational function. A chosen denominator exponent is not a divisor order: $f=z^2$, $h=z^{-1}$, $a=2$, $c=1$ has equality in (43), although a denominator representation can suggest the wrong bound.\n\nFor positive integers $r,q$ with $q/r>c/a$, $h^rf^q$ has no prime-divisor pole. Normality gives $h^rf^q\\in A$: the height-one DVR intersection property identifies regular elements with those having nonnegative orders. This is the normal-domain Hartogs algebra already contained in the accepted rational bridge; no normalization hull replaces the original $A$ hull.\n\nLet $x$ be in the $A$-polynomial hull of compact $L\\subset (\\operatorname{Spec}A_f)^{\\rm an}$, and assume $|\\log|f||\\le M$ on $L\\cup\\{x\\}$. Apply the hull inequality to $(h^rf^q)^l\\in A$. Dividing by $rl$, or directly using its logarithm, gives\n$$a\\log|h(x)|+b\\le\\sup_L(a\\log|h|+b)+2a(q/r)M.$$\nLet $q/r\\downarrow c/a$. Then\n$$a\\log|h(x)|+b\\le\\sup_L(a\\log|h|+b)+2cM. \\tag{44}$$\nIf $h(x)=0$ the assertion is immediate; if $h$ vanishes everywhere on $L$, the same polynomial forces $h(x)=0$. Hence no subtraction of infinities was used. This simplification is valid for normal $A$ and does not prove controlled AFS approximation of a weak input on $A_f$. Such approximation remains a separate premise before a localized-hull or Runge application.\n\n# Carrier envelopes: the retraction issue and raw roots\n\nWe isolate a topological lemma which avoids an openness assumption for torification. Let $q:Y\\to Y$ be a continuous retraction on a compact Hausdorff space with image $B$. For a bounded-above $U$ satisfying $U=U\\circ q$,\n$$U_Y^*|_B=(U|_B)_B^*. \\tag{45}$$\nFor any neighborhood $N$ of $b\\in B$ in $B$, the set $q^{-1}(N)$ is a neighborhood of $b$ in $Y$, and the supremum of $U$ on it is the supremum on $N$. This gives one limsup inequality. Neighborhoods in the ambient space restrict to neighborhoods in $B$, giving the other. No openness of $q$ is required.\n\nAt a rational Reeb field the accepted quotient gives residual torification $q_V$ and its compact image $B$ identified with $K$ by the calibrated Gauss lift. Splitting projective FS sections into residual weight components with their original coefficients produces an actual globally generating FS metric $\\psi\\circ q_V\\ge\\psi$. Uniform limits and actual decreasing limits preserve membership and composition. Thus if unrestricted projective EP were independently proved on this actual normal quotient, apply it to the invariant family, then torify its actual envelope. Equation (45) identifies its carrier restriction with the carrier usc supremum. This gives the invariant carrier property without any claim about a full-link envelope equality away from the image.\n\nThe raw-root transport can be checked independently. On torified points off the vertex, the accepted maps give $\\Omega\\simeq K\\times\\mathbb R$, with $v\\mapsto(s_\\xi(v),\\rho_\\xi(v))$ and inverse $(x,r)\\mapsto x-r\\xi$. An equivariant extension has usc regularization $U^*(v)=h^*(s_\\xi(v))+\\rho_\\xi(v)$. For a uniformly upper-bounded family, its supremum $U$ and $U^*$ satisfy the same uniform orbit slopes $0<c\\le C$:\n$$U(v)-Ct\\le U(v+t\\eta)\\le U(v)-ct\\quad(t\\ge0).$$\nEvery orbit is finite with a unique root or identically $-\\infty$. Bracketing the zero shows\n$$\\mathcal R(\\sup_i u_i)=\\sup_i\\mathcal R u_i,\\qquad\n \\mathcal R(U^*)=(\\mathcal R U)^*. \\tag{46}$$\nFor the second identity, a value above the root has negative $U^*$ on a neighborhood and pulls back to an upper root bound. A value below the root has every neighborhood meeting $\\{U>0\\}$ and pulls back to a lower limsup root bound. If the root is $-\\infty$, the upper argument works for every real parameter. Applying the accepted actual raw inverse then transports carrier usc-sup membership between Reeb fields. This transfers a property **after** its rational normal base is proved; it transports neither $I$ completeness nor strong topology.\n\n# What the singular source and the synthetic source leave unresolved\n\nE is the exact public preprint 2609.27610v1. Its PDF and extract SHA256 hashes, respectively, are:\n\n```text\n11808a6c9ede9c0158b27e16700fc180a4ac8df12a4586a7178e1958e8f6bba8\nc8ffe91a631da67f6d3872a1a06ab8d15755aed2f926cdf326725ca3662cef7d\n```\n\nIts printed Definition 3.15 at LF766--768 is LOWER growth; Proposition 4.19 at LF1560--1562 asserts equality with global decreasing AFS regularizability. This verdict does not complete original P13.2 or certify native publication.\n\nThe accepted P13.1 and round73 mathematical bodies are used only within their explicitly granted scope. In particular, actual classes, raw order-preserving root bijections, fixed-field products, calibrated continuous rational correspondence, weighted decreasing-net continuity, finite-target integrability, midpoint bounds and outer differentiation are available. Neither general compactness nor inverse existence is inherited from those grants. Unreviewed drafts and documentary observations supply no mathematical premises.\n\nFor n1, the supremum tests are finite on normalized actual PSH. Genuine decreasing CPSH majorants separate distinct restrictions. The energy infimum over continuous majorants proves upper semicontinuity directly: S_psi(v)<epsilon implies v<=psi+epsilon. The root threshold identity uses the actual inverse root of psi+t, which is a permissible CPSH test even when it is not normalized. Strict inequalities require uniform gaps, not merely pointwise order. A downward source shift c produces a downward target gap at least c/b; the inverse produces a gap at least ac. These bounds establish both strict sublevel and strict superlevel continuity. The resulting homeomorphism asserts no strong-I continuity or universal compactness.\n\nFor n2, T_r is a continuous self-map of the actual carrier. Orbit slopes and equivariance give |u(T_r x)-u(x)|<=2b|r| wherever u is finite, and preserve its minus-infinity locus. Consequently its Radon pushforward integrates every actual source E1 potential. The granted integrability criterion establishes finite dual energy; no MA pullback identity is needed.\n\nThe root-L1 lemma is correct for arbitrary nets. For each fixed index, integrability excludes the exceptional loci of the two potentials being compared; finite orbit slopes then control their entire finite orbits. No common null set for an uncountable net is required. With a fixed rational M and finite rational grid, the clipped-root estimate is bounded by a^(-1) times the sum of finitely many orbit-sample L1 errors, plus 2bh/a. Boundary clipping follows from the same minimum-slope inequality. The net limit is taken with that grid fixed, followed by mesh refinement. The tail inequality is |R_i-max(R_i,-M)|<=a^(-1)(-u_i-aM)_+. Source L1 convergence controls these tails at each fixed M, giving limsup ||R_i-R||_1<=2a^(-1) integral(-u-aM)_+. The cutoff tends to infinity last. Thus the lemma retains exactly its stated L1 antecedents and makes no moving-MA assertion.\n\nI checked the pertinent projective statements and proof bodies in [Boucksom–Jonsson v4](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1801.08229v4). The smooth envelope theorem applies to the actual integral, connected, smooth projective Y over the retained field and its ample L_d. Its multiplier-ideal proof supplies uniform envelope approximation on smooth test models. Normalized compactness then follows through bounded divisorial values, Tychonoff and decreasing upper-tail envelopes. Neither argument requires a countable field or metrizability. The ample-class regularization statements genuinely permit sections of powers of the original L_d; they do not replace its line-bundle reference by another numerical representative.\n\nTorification preserves that reference. Splitting globally generating sections into residual weight components, with their original coefficients, preserves global generation and produces phi composed with q, dominating phi. Uniform approximation and genuine decreasing nets extend this to CPSH and PSH. The trivial valuation remains fixed, and energy monotonicity preserves actual E1. In both directions, the unbounded rational correspondence is obtained from actual decreasing CPSH nets with a nontrivial value at the trivial valuation. Decreasing energy continuity consequently extends the calibration, including energy minus infinity.\n\nThe factors remain V_eta=s^(-n)d^(1-n)(L_d^p), omega_eta=L_d/(sd), omega_eta^p=sV_eta and E_eta=E_Ld/(sd). Projective mixed probabilities divide raw curvature by (L_d^p), not by V_eta. The bounded calibrated identities extend at this one fixed rational field through weighted decreasing nets. The limiting weight is integrable because it is E1. Support persists in the closed carrier B, so the lift through ell remains defined, preserves probability mass, and gives exactly the claimed weighted identity with factor 1/(sd).\n\nThe compactness argument avoids an unproved closure assertion for invariant potentials. The map A from the genuine compact normalized projective class is an actual surjection. For invariant Psi, sup_Y(phi composed with q-Psi)=sup_B(phi-Psi)=sup_Y(phi-Psi): restriction to B gives one inequality, and torification domination gives the other. Proposition 4.28 therefore makes A continuous for the supremum-test topology. Its Hausdorff target is compact.\n\nThe supported projective lift of a finite-energy cone probability is genuinely finite energy. Every projective E1 potential torifies to E1 and agrees with its torification on B; actual cone integrability therefore gives projective integrability, and Theorem 9.20 applies. For bounded-J cone nets, any alleged L1 failure admits a projectively convergent subnet. Energy upper semicontinuity makes its limit E1, continuity of A identifies its carrier restriction, and Proposition 9.19 contradicts the failure. This proves convergence of the whole original net. The source's signed-integration proof uses uniform approximation by model-MA pairings supported at finitely many divisorial points. Absolute L1 follows from the corrected maximum identity and bounded energy of normalized maxima, rather than from signed convergence alone.\n\nRoot homeomorphisms transfer actual compactness to every fixed xi. The inverse-root energy comparison bounds J at eta; the orbit pushforwards supply every required finite-energy target, and the finite-grid lemma transfers their L1 convergence back to xi. No unbounded currents are transported across varying Reeb fields.\n\nFor n3, the deficit bound gives J(w_j)<=(L+d_j)/(2a_p). A compact subnet has an actual PSH limit, upper semicontinuity makes its energy finite, and target L1 continuity makes the variational objective upper semicontinuous. The actual E1 dual-supremum equality supplies the opposite inequality, proving attainment. Applying the outer obstacle inequality to w+tf and using both signs of differentiation forces equality of MA and target integrals on the dense continuous direction lattice. Both measures are genuine probabilities on K, so MA_xi(w)=mu. The granted tangent identity proves necessity, and the existing embedding proves uniqueness modulo constants.\n\nThe whole-sequence claim also holds beyond the particular FS sequence chosen for attainment. For any normalized actual E1 maximizing sequence, the midpoint inequality against the attained maximizer gives I(w_j,w)<=d_j/(2a_p). The granted signed target estimate then identifies target-integral limits, and the maximizing identity identifies energy limits. No I-completeness assumption is used. For p=1, a_1=1/16 and I<=8d. For n=1, the granted effective normal grading gives k[z], the one-point carrier, E(c)=c and the calibrated radial Dirac probability; no estimate involving a_0 is applied.\n\nThe dimension-two assertion is valid. Normal integral projective curve quotients have one-dimensional Noetherian normal closed local rings, hence DVRs and regular local rings. Over the perfect retained field, regularity implies smoothness. I checked these implications in [Stacks 00PD](https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/00PD) and [Stacks 0B8X](https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0B8X).\n\nThe scalar quadric example is correct. Its invariant-ring presentation proves normality as an ungraded ring; it need not identify the covering polynomial ring's grading with the specified ordinary scalar grading. The latter grading gives a good three-dimensional cone. Its projective quotient has local equation uv=w^2, local dimension two and cotangent dimension three at the stated vertex. Every rational Reeb in the specified one-dimensional torus gives the same singular Proj. This establishes failure of the additional hypothesis, not nonexistence of MA solutions.\n\nHarmless printing corrections are explicit: the absolute-value identity is 2max(a,b)-a-b; weighted integration is the test-containing pairing minus the zero-test pairing. In checking the source dependencies, the difference-of-powers telescope includes j=0 through p-1, and the quadratic scaling factor is t[1-(1-t)^p], bounded by pt^2. These corrections follow directly from the displayed algebra and source proof expansions. The p=1 case uses direct quadratic Cauchy–Schwarz. They require no substantive repair of the round79 argument.\n\nThe n4 gap analysis is sound. On cones without a smooth rational quotient, this packet still supplies no unconditional actual compactness or realization theorem. Its proposed extension remains conditional on independently established bounded-energy compactness and finite-energy-target L1 continuity at a normal rational quotient. The projective completeness equivalence does not establish that antecedent. No general singular EP, invariant-subset closure, all-point weak convergence or actual realization on all higher-dimensional singular quotients has been assumed.",

# PRIMARY literal general-projective statement and pertinent proof

Lemma 4.26. For each ψ ∈ PL(X), there exists a finite set Σ ⊂ X div such that
                                  sup(ϕ − ψ) = max(ϕ − ψ)                                  (4.7)
                                   X an            Σ

for all ϕ ∈ PL+ (X), and the same result then holds for all ϕ ∈ PSH(θ), θ ∈ N1 (X).
Moreover, if ψ = r(ϕa − ρ), where r ∈ Q>0 , a is a flag ideal, and ρ ∈ PL+ (X), then we can
pick Σ = Σa , the set of Rees valuations of a.
Proof. We can always write ψ = r(ϕa − ρ) as above, and by homogeneity we may assume
r = 1. The case ϕ ∈ PL+ (X) now follows from Lemma 2.13, since ϕ + ρ ∈ PL+ (X).
  Now assume ϕ ∈ PSH(θ) for some θ ∈ N1 (X). After replacing θ with ω ∈ Amp(X)
such that ω − θ is nef, we may assume θ = ω is ample. By Theorem 4.15, we can find a
decreasing net (ϕi ) in Hdom (ω) ⊂ PL+ (X) such that ϕi & ϕ. For each v ∈ X an , we then
have ϕi (v) − ψ(v) ≤ maxΣa (ϕi − ψ) for all i, and hence ϕ(v) − ψ(v) ≤ maxΣa (ϕ − ψ). 
